{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{CB}
does not contain any scalars, there will be no need to renormalize the current vertices. However, we still have contributions coming from bowtie diagrams displayed in Figure
3
(b). We have:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}=\\
&{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}\\
&-\bigg[\frac{{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(-p-p_{1}){\rangle}_{FB}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}}{{\langle}O(-p-p_{1})O(p+p_{1}){\rangle}_{FB}}\par+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}\bigg]\end{aligned}

(3.23)

Our goal is to fix down the CB algebra by relating it to the FB theory. The first term on the right-hand side of (
3.23
) involves the zero-operator
(\partial\cdot J_{3})^{FB}
so it can only contribute contact terms. Therefore, any non-contact contributions must come from the bowtie diagrams. We can use the
{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}O]{\rangle}_{CB}
Ward identity and (
3.2
) to write these in a different form:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\bigg[\frac{{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(-p-p_{1}){\rangle}_{FB}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}}{{\langle}O(-p-p_{1})O(p+p_{1}){\rangle}_{FB}}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}\bigg]=\\
&\qquad\qquad\par\bigg({\langle}\delta_{O}J^{FB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(-p-p_{1}){\rangle}_{FB}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\\
&\qquad\qquad\par+\bigg({\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})\delta_{J}O^{FB}(-p-p_{1}){\rangle}_{FB}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(3.24)

In the first bracket, we have decomposed the variation of the vector current as
\delta J_{\alpha}^{FB}=\delta_{O}J_{\alpha}^{FB}+\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{FB}
with the latter vanishing inside the two-point function. We can now use the expression (
3.9
), to rewrite the terms in the first bracket as coming from the FB Ward identity:

\displaystyle{\langle}\delta_{O}J^{FB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(-p-p_{1}){\rangle}_{FB}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}=\par{\langle}\delta_{O}J^{FB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}

(3.25)

The terms in the second bracket can be written to match the terms coming from local non-conservation of the spin-3 current in (
3.22
). To do so, we employ the FB algebra (
3.8
) and the fact that the two point function of the spin-1 current remains the same in the free and critical theory, as in (
3.2
):

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})\delta_{J}O^{FB}(-p-p_{1}){\rangle}_{FB}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}=\\
&-\frac{64i}{3}\bigg(ip_{1(\mu}+\frac{3}{5}ip_{(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(-p_{1}){\rangle}_{CB}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}\end{aligned}

(3.26)

Schematically speaking, this is the same mechanism observed for the two-point function Ward identity of Section
3.1
. Going from the free to the critical theory, the variation of the scalar current changes, but this is compensated by the divergence developed by the spin-3 current. We can insert equations (
3.24
) - (
3.26
) back into (
3.23
) and use the FB algebra to write out
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{FB}
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}=\\
&{\langle}(\cancel{\delta_{O}J_{\alpha}^{FB}}+\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{FB})J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}-\cancel{{\langle}\delta_{O}J_{\alpha}^{FB}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}}\\
&+\frac{64i}{3}\bigg(ip_{1(\mu}+\frac{3}{5}ip_{(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(-p_{1}){\rangle}_{CB}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}\\
&+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}\end{aligned}

(3.27)

We can now directly compare this expression to the Ward identity (
3.22
). The only possibility to match kinematic dependences of the two equations leads to
\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{CB}=\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{FB}
and
{\tilde{b}}_{4}=0
. We find unique algebra by which all higher-spin Ward identities are consistent. Our final results for this section are summed up as:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\delta O^{CB}=\varnothing\qquad\implies[Q_{\mu\nu},O]_{CB}=\varnothing\\
&\delta J_{\alpha}^{CB}=\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{FB}\implies[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}]_{CB}=-\frac{16i}{3}\partial_{(\mu}T_{\mu)\alpha}\\
&(\partial\cdot J)^{CB}_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{N}\frac{64i}{3}\bigg(\frac{2}{5}O\partial_{(\mu}J_{\nu)}-\frac{3}{5}J_{(\mu}\partial_{\nu)}O+\frac{g_{\mu\nu}}{5}J^{\sigma}\partial_{\sigma}O\bigg)\end{aligned}

(3.28)

Where
\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{FB}
is given by (
3.10
). Of course, one should keep in mind the normalization of operators (
1.2
). Furthermore, the Ward identities also provide constraints on contact terms of the theory. If we wish to preserve higher-spin symmetry, contact terms that we add to three-point functions must satisfy (
3.15
), (
3.16
), (
3.21
) and (
3.22
). We expand on this in Section
4
.

4
Local quasifermionic Ward identities

Having understood the Ward identities in the free/critical limits, we analyze the interpolating case, that being theories of matter coupled to a Chern-Simons gauge field in three dimensions. As identified in
[
13
]
, such theories have approximate higher-spin symmetry at large
N
, even at strong coupling. There is a number of dualities relating conformal fixed points of various Chern-Simons-matter theories
[
2
,
1
,
4
]
, which can be thought of as generalizations of the level/rank duality that include matter fields. Furthermore, these field theories are interesting as holographic duals of higher-spin gravity theories in
AdS_{4}
.

We will particularly be interested in Ward identities of Quasifermionic Chern-Simons-matter theory. This theory admits multiple equivalent descriptions. The primary description is that of a Dirac fermion
\psi
in the fundamental representation of
U(N)
coupled to gluons
A_{\mu}
with Chern-Simons interactions at level
k
. The action is given by:

\displaystyle S_{FF+CS}=-\frac{ik}{8\pi}\int d^{3}x{\epsilon}^{\mu\nu\rho}\bigg(A_{\mu}^{a}\partial_{\nu}A_{\rho}^{a}+\frac{1}{3}f^{abc}A^{a}_{\mu}A^{b}_{\nu}A^{c}_{\rho}\bigg)+\int d^{3}x{\bar{\psi}}{\gamma}^{\mu}D_{\mu}{\psi}

(4.1)

At large
N
, this theory is suggested to be dual to the
U(k)
model of Legendre-transformed bosons with Chern-Simons interactions at level
-{\text{sign}}(k)N
. The duality was checked robustly by comparing three-point correlators
[
23
,
15
]
and thermal partition functions
[
14
]
. The action of the CB+CS theory at level
k_{b}
can be written as:

\displaystyle S_{CB+CS}=-\frac{ik_{b}}{8\pi}\int d^{3}x{\epsilon}^{\mu\nu\rho}\bigg(A_{\mu}^{a}\partial_{\nu}A_{\rho}^{a}+\frac{1}{3}f^{abc}A^{a}_{\mu}A^{b}_{\nu}A^{c}_{\rho}\bigg)+\int d^{3}x\bigg(|D_{\mu}{\phi}|^{2}+\sigma({\phi^{\dagger}}{\phi})\bigg)

(4.2)

Here,
\sigma
is an auxiliary field introduced by performing the Legendre transform with respect to
O^{FB}={\phi^{\dagger}}{\phi}
.

Lastly, from the holographic point of view, QF Chern-Simons-matter theory is mapped to a quantum theory of interacting higher-spin gauge fields
[
14
]
. The equations of motion for a class of such theories were written down by Vasiliev
[
25
]
. In
[
10
]
, the cubic vertices of the bulk theory were obtained by bootstrapping from higher-spin symmetry, which further suggested that in certain chiral limits one obtains a quantum-complete, local theory of higher-spin gravity. The (anti-)chiral higher-spin theories
11
11

11

These theories were analyzed in
[
26
,
27
,
28
,
29
,
30
,
31
,
32
]
and others, including flat space constructions studied in
[
33
,
34
,
35
]
.
were argued to correspond to closed subsectors of QF Chern-Simons-matter theory, although at present there is no clear CFT description. In
[
36
,
37
]
, it was argued that by analytically continuing the parameters of Chern-Simons-matter theory, one obtains a consistent match between CFT three-point functions and cubic bulk vertices.

We will analyze these theories in the ’t Hooft limit
N,k\to\infty
at finite ’t Hooft coupling
\lambda=N/k
. Even without understanding the underlying microscopic theory, the presence of weakly broken higher-spin symmetry is sufficient to constrain correlators to a two-variable family
12
12

12

The analysis also assumes a well-behaved CFT, with a specific spectrum of single-trace operators. For details, see
[
13
]
. In the case of QF Chern-Simons-matter theory, these variables were established via parameters of the theory
[
15
]
. In the (
4.1
) description, we have:

\displaystyle{\tilde{N}}=2N\frac{\sin(\pi\lambda)}{\pi\lambda},\qquad{\tilde{\lambda}}=\tan\bigg(\frac{\pi\lambda}{2}\bigg)

(4.3)

Qualitatively,
{\tilde{N}}
can be thought of as the effective number of degrees of freedom of the theory, while
{\tilde{\lambda}}
controls the level of symmetry breaking, so that at
{\tilde{\lambda}}=0
the symmetry is unbroken, and then by turning on the gauge field we increase
{\tilde{\lambda}}
, reaching the critical model in the
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to\infty
limit.

Correlators of the QF theory are often written in terms of FF and CB correlators. Note that in this section and Section
5
, we will be using a different convention than in Sections
1.1
-
3
. All QF correlators scale like
{\tilde{N}}
, which we set to one by setting
N=\frac{1}{2},\lambda\to 0
. Hence we take the subscripts
{\langle}{\rangle}_{FF}
(similarly
{\langle}{\rangle}_{CB}
) in the following sections to mean one half of the correlator in the theory of a single Dirac fermion (or complex boson), and in particular carry an extra
\frac{1}{2}
factor relative to the ones defined in Section
1.1
.

We will first summarize some known results for two- and three-point functions. The scalar two-point function remains the same as in the free theory, up to an overall factor, while two-point functions of spinning operators acquire a parity-odd contact term:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\langle}O(p)O(-p){\rangle}_{QF}=&{\tilde{N}}{\langle}O(p)O(-p){\rangle}_{FF}\\
{\langle}J_{s}(p)J_{s}(-p){\rangle}_{QF}=&{\tilde{N}}{\langle}J_{s}(p)J_{s}(-p){\rangle}_{FF}+{\tilde{N}}g_{s}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\langle}J_{s}(p)J_{s}(-p){\rangle}_{odd}\text{ for $s>0$}\end{aligned}

(4.4)

The odd piece can be written as
{\langle}J_{s}(p)J_{s}(-p){\rangle}_{odd}={\langle}(\epsilon\cdot J_{s})(p)J_{s}(-p){\rangle}_{FF}
, where
\epsilon
denotes the epsilon-transform
[
38
,
39
]
:

\displaystyle(\epsilon\cdot J)_{\mu_{1}\mu_{2}...\mu_{s}}(p)={\epsilon}_{\alpha\beta(\mu_{1}}\frac{p^{\alpha}}{p}J^{\beta}_{\mu_{2}...\mu_{s})}(p)

(4.5)

The functions
g_{s}({\tilde{\lambda}})
can apriori be any general functions of the coupling. We will only encounter contact terms of the spin-1 two-point function which were computed in
[
23
,
15
]
. Curiously, perturbative computation in the FF+CS theory implies
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})={\tilde{\lambda}}
, while the same computation in the CB+CS theory yields
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})=-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{-1}
. This suggests that the descriptions (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) as well as their gravity duals
[
10
]
are not equivalent at the level of contact terms. We explore this question further in Section
4.3

Three-point functions take the form obtained in
[
13
]
, up to contact terms. For
s_{i}>0
, in the normalization (
4.4
), we have:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\langle}J_{s_{1}}OO{\rangle}_{QF}=&{\tilde{N}}{\langle}J_{s_{1}}OO{\rangle}_{FF}={\tilde{N}}{\langle}J_{s_{1}}OO{\rangle}_{CB}\\
{\langle}J_{s_{1}}J_{s_{2}}O{\rangle}_{QF}=&\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}J_{s_{1}}J_{s_{2}}O{\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}J_{s_{1}}J_{s_{2}}O{\rangle}_{CB}\\
{\langle}J_{s_{1}}J_{s_{2}}J_{s_{3}}{\rangle}_{QF}=&\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}J_{s_{1}}J_{s_{2}}J_{s_{3}}{\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}J_{s_{1}}J_{s_{2}}J_{s_{3}}{\rangle}_{odd}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}J_{s_{1}}J_{s_{2}}J_{s_{3}}{\rangle}_{CB}\end{aligned}

(4.6)

Three-point functions not-involving the scalar current have an additional parity-odd piece, that does not appear in the free/critical limits. As was discussed in
[
39
,
40
]
, the odd piece can be related to the FF and CB pieces via the epsilon-transform (
4.5
) on one of the currents. We will highlight this in specific examples.

The QF theory breaks higher-spin symmetry at subleading order in
{\tilde{N}}
. This is evident in the CB limit when
{\tilde{\lambda}}
is taken to infinity, however it also holds at finite
{\tilde{\lambda}}
. Current operators remain conformal primaries to leading order in large
N
, therefore most generally, we have:

\displaystyle(\partial\cdot J)^{QF}_{\mu\nu}=\tilde{F}({\tilde{\lambda}})(\partial\cdot J)^{CB}_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{{\tilde{N}}}\frac{64i}{3}F({\tilde{\lambda}})\bigg(\frac{2}{5}O\partial_{(\mu}J_{\nu)}-\frac{3}{5}J_{(\mu}\partial_{\nu)}O+\frac{g_{\mu\nu}}{5}J^{\sigma}\partial_{\sigma}O\bigg)

(4.7)

For some function
F({\tilde{\lambda}})
satisfying
F(0)=0
and
F(\infty)=1
. We know that
F({\tilde{\lambda}})\neq 0
for finite
{\tilde{\lambda}}
, since the only theories with exact higher-spin symmetry are free theories
[
12
]
. Indeed, by considering classical equations of motion of the theory
[
14
]
, it was derived that
F({\tilde{\lambda}})={\tilde{\lambda}}\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}^{-1}
up to possible subleading corrections. As we will see, the Ward identity analysis consistently constrains this to be the full result to all orders in
\lambda
.

One of our tasks for this section is to constrain the higher-spin algebra of QF Chern-Simons-matter theory, confining ourselves to operators with spin
s<4
. We assume that the variations of operators interpolate smoothly between free and critical limits. In the FF theory, we have fixed the action of the spin-3 pseudocharge on scalar and vector currents as (
2.15
) and (
2.17
). This constrains the local variations up to a linear combinations of local contact terms. Under the insertion of
(\partial\cdot J)^{FF}_{\mu\nu}(x)
, the variation of the scalar current
O(x_{1})
and the vector current
J_{\alpha}(x_{2})
are given by:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\delta O^{FF}(x_{1})=\frac{8}{3}{\epsilon}_{\rho_{1}\rho_{2}(\mu}\bigg(w_{1}^{FF}\delta(x-x_{1})\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\rho_{1}}-w_{2}^{FF}\partial_{\nu)}\delta(x-x_{1})\partial^{\rho_{1}}-w_{2}^{FF}\partial^{\rho_{1}}\delta(x-x_{1})\partial_{\nu)}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(1-w_{1}^{FF}-2w_{2}^{FF})\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\rho_{1}}\delta(x-x_{1})\bigg)J^{\rho_{2}}(x)\\[15.0pt]
&\delta J_{\alpha}^{FF}(x_{2})=\delta_{O}J_{\alpha}^{FF}(x_{2})+\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{FF}(x_{2})\\[5.0pt]
&\delta_{O}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(x_{2})=-\frac{4}{3}{\epsilon}_{\alpha\rho(\mu}\bigg(w_{3}^{FF}\delta(x-x_{2})\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\rho}-w_{4}^{FF}\partial^{\rho}\delta(x-x_{2})\partial_{\nu)}-w_{4}^{FF}\partial_{\nu)}\delta(x-x_{2})\partial^{\rho}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+(1-w_{3}^{FF}-2w_{4}^{FF})\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\rho}\delta(x-x_{2})\bigg)O(x)\\
&\delta_{T}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(x_{2})=\frac{16i}{3}\delta(x_{2}-x)\partial_{(\mu}T_{\nu)\alpha}(x)+w_{5}^{FF}\bigg(\delta(x_{2}-x)\partial_{\alpha}+\partial_{\alpha}\delta(x_{2}-x)\bigg)T_{\mu\nu}(x)\end{aligned}

(4.8)

The parameters
w_{1-5}^{FF}
could in principle be fixed by calculating various FF correlators involving
(\partial\cdot J_{3})^{FF}
and insertions of spin-0,1,2 currents, which we leave as a topic of future work. In the critical limit, these expressions should go over smoothly to the variations of the critical theory:
\delta O^{FF}\to\delta O^{CB}
and
\delta J_{\alpha}^{FF}\to\delta J_{\alpha}^{CB}
, which were determined in (
3.10
) and (
3.28
) up to an unfixed constant
w_{1}^{FB}
. We can now write a general ansatz for the algebra of the QF theory as:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\delta O^{QF}(x_{1})=f_{0}({\tilde{\lambda}})\delta O^{FF}(x_{1})+h_{0}({\tilde{\lambda}})\delta O^{int}(x_{1})\\[10.0pt]
&\delta J_{\alpha}^{QF}(x_{1})=f_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})\delta_{O}J_{\alpha}^{FF}(x_{1})+f_{2}({\tilde{\lambda}})\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{FF}(x_{1})+f_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{CB}(x_{1})\\[5.0pt]
&\qquad\quad\par+\bigg(h_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})\delta_{O}J_{\alpha}^{int}(x_{1})+h_{2}({\tilde{\lambda}})\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{int}(x_{1})+\frac{h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{{\tilde{N}}}\delta(x_{1}-x){\epsilon}_{\alpha\rho(\mu}J_{\nu)}J^{\rho}(x)\bigg)\end{aligned}

(4.9)

Here we have also included the possible intermediate terms that can appear at finite
{\tilde{\lambda}}
but vanish in the free/critical limits.
\delta O^{int}
is such a contribution in the variation of the QF scalar and can have the form similar to
\delta O^{FF}
in (
4.8
), but possibly with different coefficients. Similarly,
\delta J_{\alpha}^{QF}
can have intermediate terms
\delta_{O}J_{\alpha}^{int}
proportional to the scalar current and terms
\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{int}
proportional to the stress-tensor, which are similar to their FF counterparts in (
4.8
), with possibly different coefficients. Furthermore,
\delta J_{\alpha}^{QF}
may now contain a double-trace contribution of two spin-1 currents, as explicitly written in (
4.9
). Note that in principle each monomial in the intermediate variations could have appeared with a different function of
{\tilde{\lambda}}
, however as we will see, these functions are strongly constrained by the higher-spin Ward identities. Anticipating this, we’ve written down the ansatz with certain terms grouped, and general functions
h_{i}({\tilde{\lambda}})
that vanish as
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0,\infty
. We expect the functions
f_{i}({\tilde{\lambda}})
to satisfy:

\displaystyle\lim_{{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0}f_{0}({\tilde{\lambda}})=1\qquad\lim_{{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0}f_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})=1\qquad\lim_{{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0}f_{2}({\tilde{\lambda}})=1\qquad\lim_{{\tilde{\lambda}}\to\infty}f_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})=1

(4.10)

With all other
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0,\infty
limits vanishing. Based on these considerations, the spin-3 pseudocharge should act on
O
and
J_{\alpha}
as:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &[Q_{\mu\nu},O]_{QF}=\bigg(f_{0}({\tilde{\lambda}})+h_{0}({\tilde{\lambda}})\bigg){\epsilon}_{\alpha\beta(\mu}\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\alpha}J^{\beta}\\[10.0pt]
&[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}]_{QF}=\bigg(f_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})+h_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})\bigg){\epsilon}_{\sigma\alpha(\mu}\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\sigma}O+\bigg(f_{2}({\tilde{\lambda}})+f_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})+h_{2}({\tilde{\lambda}})\bigg)\partial_{(\mu}T_{\nu)\alpha}\\[5.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+h_{2}({\tilde{\lambda}})\partial_{\alpha}T_{\mu\nu}+\frac{h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{{\tilde{N}}}{\epsilon}_{\alpha\rho(\mu}J_{\nu)}J^{\rho}\end{aligned}

(4.11)

In Section
4.1
, we fix the unknown functions by analyzing local Ward identities of the spin-3 current in the QF theory. We will use these results to constrain four-point functions in Section
4.2
and discuss higher-spin contact terms in Section
4.3
.

4.1
Fixing the QF algebra

The simplest correlator to consider is the three-point function of involving the scalar, vector and spin-3 currents. As higher-spin symmetry is broken, we find contributions from both the divergence of the spin-3 current (
4.7
) and the local QF algebra (
4.9
). In position space, the higher-spin Ward identity reads:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}=\par{\langle}\delta J^{QF}_{\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}+{\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})\delta O^{QF}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}\\
&+\frac{1}{{\tilde{N}}}\frac{64i}{3}F({\tilde{\lambda}})\bigg(-\frac{2}{5}\partial_{1(\mu}+\frac{3}{5}\partial_{2(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})J_{\nu)}(x){\rangle}_{QF}{\langle}O(x)O(x_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}\end{aligned}

(4.12)

We compare this with the form of the three-point function
{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}O{\rangle}_{QF}
implied by higher-spin symmetry. As in (
4.6
), up to possible contact terms, we can decompose the correlator into FF and CB pieces. Then, by taking the divergence of the spin-3 current, we have:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}=\\
&\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2}){\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2}){\rangle}_{CB}\end{aligned}

(4.13)

We find that the QF Ward identity reduces to a combination of the FF and CB Ward identities, which were analyzed in Sections
2.4.1
and
3.1
. We can rewrite this as:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}=\\[5.0pt]
&\bigg(\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}\delta J^{FF}_{\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2}){\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})\delta O^{FF}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{FF}\bigg)\\
&+\bigg(\cancel{\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}\delta J^{CB}_{\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2}){\rangle}_{CB}}+\cancel{\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})\delta O^{CB}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{CB}}\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64i}{3}\bigg(-\frac{2}{5}\partial_{1(\mu}+\frac{3}{5}\partial_{2(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})J_{\nu)}(x){\rangle}_{CB}{\langle}O(x)O(x_{2}){\rangle}_{CB}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(4.14)

As discussed in Section
3
, the contributions from the CB algebra vanish in this case, since
\delta O^{CB}=0
and terms coming from
\delta J^{CB}
would appear in the equation as
{\langle}TO{\rangle}_{CB}=0
. The expression (
4.14
) should match the Ward identity (
4.12
) both in contact terms and non-contact terms. We postpone the discussion of contact terms to Section
4.3
and here focus only on matching pieces that remain non-zero for
x\neq x_{1}\neq x_{2}
. In (
4.12
), the terms appearing on the right-hand side involve only two-point functions. Using (
4.4
), we can rewrite the algebra terms as as FF correlators, while the disconnected term coming from the local divergence developed by the spin-3 current can be written as a product of of CB correlators. It is straightforward then to check that the only way to match kinematic dependences between (
4.12
) and (
4.14
) is to have the higher-spin algebra pieces and the disconnected pieces match among themselves. Comparing to (
4.9
), factors of
{\tilde{N}}
drop out and we find expressions for the unknown functions (we suppress the arguments for brevity):

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &f_{0}({\tilde{\lambda}})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}},\;h_{0}({\tilde{\lambda}})=0\implies\delta O^{QF}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\delta O^{FF}\\
&f_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}},\;h_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})=0\implies\delta J^{QF}_{\alpha}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\delta_{O}J^{FF}_{\alpha}+\delta_{T}J^{QF}+\frac{h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{{\tilde{N}}}\delta(x_{1}-x){\epsilon}_{\alpha\rho(\mu}J_{\nu)}J^{\rho}\\
&F({\tilde{\lambda}})=\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\end{aligned}

(4.15)

Here we have collectively denoted all the stress-tensor terms that appear in the second line of (
4.9
) by
\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{QF}
. It is interesting that after turning on the gauge-field the variation of the scalar current remains the same as in free theory, up to a
{\tilde{\lambda}}
-dependent factor. Similarly, the scalar contact terms in the variation of the vector current only get rescaled, with no new terms appearing at finite
{\tilde{\lambda}}
. Finally, note that the result for
F({\tilde{\lambda}})
matches the one derived in
[
14
]
, and is valid to all orders in
\lambda=N/k
.

To further constrain the QF algebra, we write out the
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}J_{\alpha}T_{\beta\gamma}{\rangle}_{QF}
Ward identity, using (
4.7
) and (
4.9
):

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})T_{\beta\gamma}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}=\\[5.0pt]
&{\langle}\delta J_{\alpha}^{QF}(x_{1})T_{\beta\gamma}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}+{\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})\delta T_{\beta\gamma}^{QF}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}\\
&+\frac{1}{{\tilde{N}}}\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64i}{3}{\langle}\big(\frac{2}{5}O\partial_{(\mu}J_{\nu)}-\frac{3}{5}J_{(\mu}\partial_{\nu)}O+\frac{g_{\mu\nu}}{5}J^{\sigma}\partial_{\sigma}O\big)(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})T_{\beta\gamma}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{CB}\end{aligned}

(4.16)

The contribution from the divergence of the spin-3 current is written in the second line, and at leading order in
{\tilde{N}}
contributes as a product of two-point functions. It is easy to see that this term vanishes since the only contractions we can make in this case are
{\langle}JJ{\rangle}_{QF}{\langle}TO{\rangle}_{QF}
and
{\langle}JT{\rangle}_{QF}{\langle}JO{\rangle}_{QF}
, however these terms vanish since two-point functions of different spin currents are zero. Therefore the right-hand side of (
4.16
) only contains contributions from the QF algebra.

The first term is a sum of contact terms going as
\delta(x-x_{1})
while the second term is proportional to
\delta(x-x_{2})
, hence we can be sure that the two contributions do not cancel between themselves for
x_{1}\neq x_{2}
. One can, for example, integrate the equation around a small region around
x_{1}
, not containing
x_{2}
. This means that the
\delta T^{QF}(x_{2})
terms can be put to zero. Furthermore, as
{\langle}J_{s}J_{s^{\prime}}{\rangle}_{QF}\propto\delta_{ss^{\prime}}
, the remaining terms on the right-hand side are those proportional to
\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{QF}(x_{1})
. The key point point is that the
{\tilde{\lambda}}
-dependence on the left-hand side is given by:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})T_{\beta\gamma}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}=\\
&\frac{1}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})T_{\beta\gamma}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})T_{\beta\gamma}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{odd}\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(x)J_{\alpha}(x_{1})T_{\beta\gamma}(x_{2}){\rangle}_{CB}\end{aligned}

(4.17)

And comparing this to the ansatz (
4.9
) and (
4.10
) leads to:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &f_{2}({\tilde{\lambda}})=\frac{1}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}},\qquad f_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})=\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}};\qquad h_{2}({\tilde{\lambda}})\propto\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\end{aligned}

(4.18)

The fact that
h_{2}({\tilde{\lambda}})
is an odd function leads to a strong constraint on the terms in (
4.9
) involving the stress-tensor, due to discrete symmetries of the theory. The action (
4.1
) is invariant under a parity transformation
\vec{x}\to-\vec{x}
combined with
\lambda\to-\lambda
. As we are working in Euclidean signature, applying parity essentially contributes a minus sign in correlators for each Lorentz index
13
13

13

Note that the quasifermionic scalar transforms as
O\to-O
under parity.
. Requiring that the Ward identity
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}J_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
remains invariant under the combined transformation implies that
\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{int}
terms must be parity even, however conformal invariance and Lorentz symmetry restrict such terms from appearing, and therefore
\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{int}=0
. Similarly, the invariance of the
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}J_{\gamma}{\rangle}_{QF}
Ward identity constrains
h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})
to be even in
{\tilde{\lambda}}
. We will revisit these Ward identities in Sections
4.2
.

Inserting these results back into (
4.9
), we fix the local algebra of the QF theory, up to the constants
w_{i}^{FF,FB}
appearing in the variations of operators, as well as a single unknown function
h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})
which vanishes in the free and critical limits. The action of the spin-3 pseudocharge on the scalar and vector currents now becomes:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &[Q_{\mu\nu},O]_{QF}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{8}{3}{\epsilon}_{\alpha\beta(\mu}\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\alpha}J^{\beta}\\
&[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}]_{QF}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{4}{3}{\epsilon}_{\sigma\alpha(\mu}\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\sigma}O+\frac{16i}{3}\frac{1-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\partial_{(\mu}T_{\nu)\alpha}+\frac{h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{{\tilde{N}}}{\epsilon}_{\alpha\rho(\mu}J_{\nu)}J^{\rho}\end{aligned}

(4.19)

Where
h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})
is an even function, which could be fixed by analyzing Ward identities of the QF theory involving higher correlators. The double-trace term in
[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}]_{QF}
does not contribute in Ward identities of three-point functions at large
{\tilde{N}}
. It appears when discussing Ward identities of higher-point functions.

It is interesting to note that at the special point
{\tilde{\lambda}}=1
, the stress-tensor terms vanish from the integrated equations (
4.19
). This does not necessarily imply that these terms vanish in local variations (
4.9
), however it does suggest that that quasifermionic
n
-point functions
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}..J_{1}O..O{\rangle}_{QF}
at
{\tilde{\lambda}}=1
may be expressed purely in terms of correlators of scalar and vector currents. It would be interesting to explore this further.

4.2
Results for four-point functions

After constraining the form of the higher-spin algebra, we can obtain non-trivial relations in correlators with an insertion of
(\partial\cdot J_{3})^{QF}
. Similarly to the case of three-point functions, higher-spin symmetry also constrains higher correlators to a tractable amount of different kinematic structures, each coming with a specific
{\tilde{\lambda}}
dependence. For correlators of QF Chern-Simons-matter theory, in the
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0
limit, we expect to recover only the FF structure, while in the
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to\infty
limit, only the CB structure survives. However, at finite
{\tilde{\lambda}}
several structures can appear. In the case of four-point functions, connections between different structures have been established in special kinematic regimes
[
41
]
and it would be interesting to check if finite
{\tilde{\lambda}}
terms can generally be constrained in terms of free theory structures, as conjectured in
[
38
]
. In this section we use weakly broken higher-spin symmetry to derive constraints on
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
and
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
.

\underline{\bm{{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}OO]{\rangle}_{QF}}\textbf{ Ward identity}}

The higher-spin Ward identity for
{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}
can be written out in momentum space using the divergence of the spin-3 current in the QF theory (
4.7
) and the QF algebra (
4.9
). We find:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}=\\
&\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64i}{3}\bigg[\par-i\bigg(p_{1(\mu}+\frac{3}{5}p_{(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\nu)}(-p_{1})J_{\alpha}(p_{1}){\rangle}_{QF}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad\par+\bigg(i\bigg(p_{2(\mu}+\frac{2}{5}p_{(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}O(-p_{2})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(p+p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}+\begin{Bmatrix}2\leftrightarrow 3\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\bigg]\\
&+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}\delta_{O}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}+\frac{1}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{CB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}\par\par+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\bigg({\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})\delta O^{FF}(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}+\begin{Bmatrix}2\leftrightarrow 3\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(4.20)

We’ve disregarded the double-trace term in
\delta J_{\alpha}^{QF}
, since it doesn’t contribute at leading order in
{\tilde{N}}
. To simplify the discussion, we also disregard terms proportional to
{\langle}OOO{\rangle}_{QF}
and
{\langle}\delta_{O}J_{\alpha}^{QF}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
as these terms do not depend on
p_{2,3}
and hence should also correspond to contact terms in
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
. These contact terms are arbitrary and can be modified by adding local counterterms to the action.

Furthermore, we will separate out terms proportional to the Chern-Simons contact term of the vector current two-point function
{\langle}JJ{\rangle}_{odd}
, as these will be analyzed separately in Section
4.3
. Using the form of three-point functions (
4.6
), the four-point function can now be decomposed into free/critical theory pieces as:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}|_{non-CS\;terms}=\\
&\quad\par\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\bigg[\bigg({\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})\delta O^{FF}(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}+\begin{Bmatrix}2\leftrightarrow 3\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)+{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}\bigg]\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\bigg[\bigg({\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})\delta O^{FF}(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}+\begin{Bmatrix}2\leftrightarrow 3\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\\
&\qquad\quad\par+\bigg(-\frac{64}{3}\bigg(p_{2(\mu}+\frac{2}{5}p_{(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}O(-p_{2})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{FF}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(p+p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}+\begin{Bmatrix}2\leftrightarrow 3\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\bigg]\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\bigg[{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{CB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}\\
&\qquad\quad+\bigg(-\frac{64}{3}\bigg(p_{2(\mu}+\frac{2}{5}p_{(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}O(-p_{2})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{CB}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(p+p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}+\begin{Bmatrix}2\leftrightarrow 3\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\bigg]\\
&=\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}J_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}J_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{odd}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}J_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{CB}\end{aligned}

(4.21)

In the
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0,\infty
limits, the Ward identity reduces to those of the free and critical theories, respectively. The four-point function
{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}
is constrained by higher-spin symmetry to three structures, which can be written out only in terms of FF and CB correlators. We also note that three point functions appearing in (
4.21
) satsify simple relations in momentum space (see Appendix
C
, as well as
[
39
]
):

\displaystyle{\langle}(\epsilon\cdot J)_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}

\displaystyle={\langle}(J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}

(4.22)

\displaystyle{\langle}T_{\mu\nu}OO{\rangle}_{FF}

\displaystyle={\langle}T_{\mu\nu}OO{\rangle}_{CB}

(4.23)

Given these simplifications, it is interesting to check if there exist additional relations between the structures of
{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}J_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
, similar to the ones observed
[
41
]
. It was argued in
[
38
]
that the correlator
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
contains three structures appearing with the same
{\tilde{\lambda}}
dependence as in (
4.21
), and conjectured that the intermediate structure satisfies an epsilon-transform relation in momentum space:

\displaystyle{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{odd}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\propto}}{\langle}({\epsilon}\cdot J)_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{FF-CB}\par\quad\text{ or }\quad\par{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{odd}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle?}}{{\propto}}{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}({\epsilon}\cdot J)_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{FF-CB}

(4.24)

Using the Ward identity (
4.21
) we may check this conjecture indirectly. We find a combination of structures that does not contain any non-local terms:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &ip^{\rho}\cdot\bigg({\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}-{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}(p)(\epsilon\cdot J)_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF-CB}\bigg)=\\
&{\langle}\delta_{T}({\epsilon}\cdot J)^{CB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}-{\langle}\delta_{T}({\epsilon}\cdot J)^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}=\text{contact terms}\end{aligned}

(4.25)

The terms coming from the divergence of
J_{3}
cancel out, and we find that the right-hand side is only a combination of contact terms coming from the higher-spin algebra
14
14

14

After writing out the local variations
\delta_{T}({\epsilon}\cdot J)^{FF,CB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})
, it is clear that the right-hand side is a function of
p+p_{1}
, which is equal to
-p_{2}-p_{3}
by momentum conservation, and thus it only depends on
p_{2}
and
p_{3}
.
. Although there is no unique way to invert the divergence in equation (
4.25
), this analysis is consistent with the epsilon-transform (
4.24
) with respect to the vector current, up to possible contact terms in
p,p_{1}
.

Furthermore, this analysis explicitly excludes the epsilon-transform (
4.24
) with respect to the spin-3 current, as after acting on it by the divergence
ip^{\rho}
and using the property
(ip\cdot({\epsilon}\cdot J_{3}(p)))_{\mu\nu}=\frac{2}{3}({\epsilon}\cdot(ip\cdot J_{3}))_{\mu\nu}
, we find that the non-contact terms of the two sides do not match.

\underline{\bm{{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}J_{\gamma}]{\rangle}_{QF}}\textbf{ Ward identity}}

Using the divergence of the spin-3 current (
4.7
) and the higher-spin algebra (
4.9
) we analyze the Ward identity involving the correlator of a spin-3 current with three vector currents:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}=\\
&{\tilde{N}}\bigg[\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64}{3}\bigg(p_{1(\mu}+\frac{3}{5}p_{(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(-p_{1}){\rangle}_{QF}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}\\
&\quad\par+\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}\delta_{O}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}+\frac{1}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}\\
&\quad\par+\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{int}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}+\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{CB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}\\
&\quad\par+h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})\bigg({\epsilon}_{\alpha\rho(\mu}{\langle}J_{\nu)}(-p_{2})J_{\beta}(p_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}{\langle}J^{\rho}(p+p_{1}+p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}+\begin{Bmatrix}\nu)\leftrightarrow\rho\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\bigg]\\
&+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}\end{aligned}

(4.26)

The correlators appearing on the right-hand side of (
4.26
) are of the form
{\langle}JJ{\rangle}_{QF}
,
{\langle}JJO{\rangle}_{QF}
and
{\langle}TJJ{\rangle}_{QF}
. Each can be separated into FF and CB theory structures as in (
4.4
) and (
4.6
). The three-point function
{\langle}TJJ{\rangle}_{QF}
contains an additional parity-odd structure which vanishes in the free/critical limits, however as discussed in Section
5.1
, we can relate it via an epsilon-transform to FF and CB pieces as (
5.11
). Thus we can decompose (
4.26
) into purely free theory structures.

We again separate out terms proportional to the Chern-Simons term
{\langle}JJ{\rangle}_{odd}
, as these will be analyzed separately in Section
4.3
. The remaining terms can be grouped as follows:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}|_{non-CS\;terms}=\\
&\quad\par\frac{1}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}\bigg[{\langle}\delta_{O}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}+{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}\bigg]\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}\bigg[{\langle}\delta_{O}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}+{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{odd}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{64}{3}\bigg(\frac{3}{5}p_{(\mu}+p_{1(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(-p_{1}){\rangle}_{FF}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}\bigg]\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}\bigg[{\langle}\delta_{O}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}+{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{CB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}\\[5.0pt]
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{64}{3}\bigg(\frac{3}{5}p_{(\mu}+p_{1(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(-p_{1}){\rangle}_{CB}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}\bigg]\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{3}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}\bigg[{\langle}\delta_{O}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}+{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{CB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{odd}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{64}{3}\bigg(\frac{3}{5}p_{(\mu}+p_{1(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(-p_{1}){\rangle}_{FF}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}\bigg]\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{\lambda}}^{4}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}\bigg[{\langle}\delta_{T}J^{CB}_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{64}{3}\bigg(\frac{3}{5}p_{(\mu}+p_{1(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(-p_{1}){\rangle}_{CB}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}\bigg]\\
&+{\tilde{N}}h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})\bigg({\epsilon}_{\alpha\rho(\mu}{\langle}J_{\nu)}(-p_{2})J_{\beta}(p_{2}){\rangle}_{CB}{\langle}J^{\rho}(p+p_{1}+p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}+\begin{Bmatrix}\nu)\leftrightarrow\rho\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\\
&+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}\end{aligned}

(4.27)

In the
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0,\infty
limits, we again recover free/critical theory Ward identities respectively, since
h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})\to 0
.

An initial analysis of the
{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}J_{\gamma}{\rangle}_{QF}
four-point function was carried out in
[
38
]
, suggesting that the correlator contains three or more structures at separated points. In (
4.27
), we observe five structures, and possibly an additional disconnected term, depending on the unfixed function
h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})
. Curiously, not all the structures are independent, as we notice a specific combination that does not contain any non-contact terms. We name the five structures in (
4.27
) appearing with factors
{\tilde{\lambda}}^{i}(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{-2}
for
i=0,1,2,3,4
by subscripts
FF,X_{1},X_{2},X_{3}
, and
CB
, respectively. Using
(ip\cdot({\epsilon}\cdot J_{3}(p)))_{\mu\nu}=\frac{2}{3}({\epsilon}\cdot(ip\cdot J_{3}))_{\mu\nu}
, we find:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &ip^{\rho}\cdot\bigg({\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF+CB-X_{2}}-{\langle}({\epsilon}\cdot J)_{\mu\nu\rho}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{X_{1}-X_{3}}\bigg)=\\
&{\langle}(\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{FF}-\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{CB})(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF-CB}-\frac{2}{3}{\langle}({\epsilon}\cdot(\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{FF}-\delta_{T}J_{\alpha}^{CB}))(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{odd}\\
&+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}=\text{contact terms}\end{aligned}

(4.28)

By expanding the local variations (
3.10
), we can confirm that the first term on the right-hand side is a function of
p+p_{1}=-p_{2}-p_{3}
and thus a contact term, depending only on
p_{2},p_{3}
, and similarly for the other two permutations. Thus it is possible, even when not acting by the divergence
ip^{\rho}
, that the left-hand side of (
4.28
) vanishes up to contact terms. Indeed, we confirm this in Section
5.3
, for collinear momenta and specific Lorentz indices, by direct perturbative calculation. It is worth noting that a similar (and in fact, slightly stronger) property was observed in the collinear limit in
[
41
]
for the five linearly-dependent structures of
{\langle}J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
.

The main results of this section are the momentum space decomposition of four-point functions (
4.21
) and (
4.27
) into free theory structures, which further suggest possible epsilon-transform relations for
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
and
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
:

\displaystyle{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{odd}={\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}({\epsilon}\cdot J)_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{FF-CB}+\text{contact terms}

(4.29)

\displaystyle{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}J_{\gamma}{\rangle}_{FF+CB-X_{2}}={\langle}({\epsilon}\cdot J)_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}J_{\gamma}{\rangle}_{X_{1}-X_{3}}+\text{contact terms}

(4.30)

Our results are partially consistent with the conjectures of
[
38
]
. Following the approach of
[
36
,
37
]
, we emphasize that the form of
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
and
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
further simplifies in spinor-helicity variables, as is often the case for two- and three-point functions of massless fields.

4.3
Relations between contact terms of QF (and QB) theories

Contact terms are terms appearing within correlation functions when two or more operator insertions coincide, reflecting the short distance physics of the theory. Most commonly, such terms are arbitrary, and can be changed by imposing a different regularization scheme or adding suitable counterterms to the action. In some cases however, contact terms hold physical significance, highlighting an underlying feature of the theory. A typical example of this is the seagull term of scalar QED, which is required by gauge invariance. In the case of Chern-Simons-matter theories, we encounter a third class of contact terms that is neither fully universal, nor arbitrary
[
17
]
. As mentioned in (
4.4
), two-point functions of spinning currents develop conformally invariant contact terms at finite
{\tilde{\lambda}}
. We encounter such a term in our analysis of higher-spin Ward identities, as
{\langle}J_{\mu}(p)J_{\nu}(-p){\rangle}_{QF}\propto\kappa_{1}({\tilde{N}},{\tilde{\lambda}}){\epsilon}_{\mu\nu\rho}p^{\rho}
. This term corresponds to a background Chern-Simons term in the generating functional of the theory. Following the discussion of
[
17
]
, one can only shift the value of this contact term by integer multiples, hence the fractional part of
\kappa_{1}({\tilde{N}},{\tilde{\lambda}})
is a physical observable. Note that in (
4.4
), we have defined the function
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})
to be the leading contribution to
\kappa_{1}({\tilde{N}},{\tilde{\lambda}})
in the large
{\tilde{N}}
expansion, therefore the function
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})
by itself does not define the observable
(\kappa_{1}({\tilde{N}},{\tilde{\lambda}})\mod 1)
.

To understand the appearance of new contact terms within QF correlators, it is instructive to work in the description (
4.2
). Equivalently, we can reach this model by starting from the theory of a free boson coupled to Chern-Simons gauge interactions and Legendre transforming it with respect to the scalar current. The CFT pre-Legendre transforming is referred to as the quasibosonic (QB) Chern-Simons-matter theory, studied in
[
23
,
42
]
. It is also constrained by approximate higher-spin symmetry, and its two-point functions of spinning currents are analogous to those of the QF theory
15
15

15

The kinematic form of these propagators is constrained by conformal invariance, however the appearing contact terms in principle have a general dependence
\tilde{g}_{s}({\tilde{\lambda}}_{qb})
on some new higher-spin symmetry variable
{\tilde{\lambda}}_{qb}
.
(
4.4
).

Figure 4
:
A generic diagram contributing to
{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}O{\rangle}_{QF}
in the CB+CS description (
4.2
). The encircled crosses denote vertices in the QB theory, the straight lines denote full boson propagators and the squiggly lines denote gluon propagators. The scalar vertex receives corrections, similarly to the CB theory vertex. The diagrams are summed over all possible gluon line configurations and scalar bubbles, as well as permutations of external vertices.

One can now ask how contact terms of associated QB and QF theories are related. The Legendre transform generally introduces new non-contact and contact terms through multiplication in momentum space (
3.2
). We start from the simplest Ward identity (
4.12
), noting that the correlator
{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}
receives new contact terms at finite
{\tilde{\lambda}}
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}|_{CS\;terms}={\tilde{N}}\frac{g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})\delta O^{FF}(p_{2}){\rangle}_{odd}\\
&+{\tilde{N}}\frac{g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64i}{3}\bigg(-\frac{2}{5}ip_{1(\mu}+\frac{3}{5}ip_{2(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\nu)}(p+p_{2}){\rangle}_{odd}{\langle}O(-p_{2})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{CB}\end{aligned}

(4.31)

After expanding the variation of the scalar (
4.8
) and the form of two-point functions, we find:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}|_{CS\;terms}=\\
&-\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{8}{3}\bigg(\par p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}p_{1\alpha}-w_{1}^{FF}p_{1(\mu}p_{\nu)}p_{\alpha}+(w_{1}^{FF}+w_{2}^{FF})(p_{1(\mu}p_{1\nu)}p_{\alpha}+p_{1(\mu}p_{\nu)}p_{1\alpha})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\par+\delta_{\alpha(\mu}\big(-p_{1\nu)}p_{1}^{2}-w_{1}^{FF}p_{\nu)}p\cdot p_{1}-(w_{1}^{FF}+w_{2}^{FF})(p_{\nu)}p_{1}^{2}+p_{1\nu)}p\cdot p_{1})\big)\bigg)\\
&-\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64i}{3}{\epsilon}_{\alpha\sigma(\mu}p_{2}\bigg(\frac{2i}{5}p_{1\nu)}p_{1}^{\sigma}-\frac{3i}{5}p_{2\nu)}p_{1}^{\sigma}-\text{Trace}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(4.32)

The corresponding QB correlator satisfies:

\displaystyle{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}={\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{QB}{\langle}O(p_{2})O(-p_{2}){\rangle}_{QB}^{-1}

(4.33)

As in Section
3
, this expression comes from summing Feynman diagrams, displayed in Figure
4
. We can invert the relation without ambiguity, to find that the QB correlator also contains contact terms. We find
16
16

16

The scalar two-point function of the quasibosonic theory satisfies
{\langle}O(p)O(-p){\rangle}_{QB}={\tilde{N}}{\langle}O(p)O(-p){\rangle}_{FB}={\tilde{N}}\frac{8}{p}
.
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{QB}|_{CS\;terms}=\\
&-\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64}{3}\frac{1}{p_{2}}\bigg(\par p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}p_{1\alpha}-w_{1}^{FF}p_{1(\mu}p_{\nu)}p_{\alpha}+(w_{1}^{FF}+w_{2}^{FF})(p_{1(\mu}p_{1\nu)}p_{\alpha}+p_{1(\mu}p_{\nu)}p_{1\alpha})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\par+\delta_{\alpha(\mu}\big(-p_{1\nu)}p_{1}^{2}-w_{1}^{FF}p_{\nu)}p\cdot p_{1}-(w_{1}^{FF}+w_{2}^{FF})(p_{\nu)}p_{1}^{2}+p_{1\nu)}p\cdot p_{1})\big)\bigg)\\
&-\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64i}{3}{\epsilon}_{\alpha\sigma(\mu}p_{2}\bigg(\frac{16i}{5}p_{1\nu)}p_{1}^{\sigma}-\frac{24i}{5}p_{2\nu)}p_{1}^{\sigma}-\text{Trace}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(4.34)

With the first term being a contact term in
p_{1}
and the second term being a contact term in
p_{2}
. Going a step further, we argue that the contributions in (
4.31
) and (
4.34
) correspond to honest contact terms in
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}O{\rangle}_{QF,QB}
. It is obvious that these terms do not appear in the free and critical limits of QB and QF theories, where
{\langle}JJ{\rangle}_{odd}=0
, and therefore do not arise as a result of the divergence acting on a non-contact term in the free/critical boson/fermion theory. However, since the non-contact pieces in
{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}O{\rangle}_{QF,QB}
are fully determined by their free and critical structures
[
13
]
, the contact terms (
4.31
), (
4.34
) must result from acting on a contact term of this correlator by
ip^{\rho}
. There is no unique way to invert this statement and obtain expressions for contact terms of
{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}O{\rangle}_{QF,QB}
, other than direct perturbative calculation. Nevertheless, we can present an example of how such contact terms could look like:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}|_{CS\;terms}=\\
&\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\bigg[i\frac{8}{3}\frac{p_{\rho}}{3p^{2}}\bigg(\par p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}p_{1\alpha}-w_{1}^{FF}p_{1(\mu}p_{\nu)}p_{\alpha}+(w_{1}^{FF}+w_{2}^{FF})(p_{1(\mu}p_{1\nu)}p_{\alpha}+p_{1(\mu}p_{\nu)}p_{1\alpha})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\par+i\frac{p_{\rho}}{3p^{2}}\delta_{\alpha(\mu}\big(-p_{1\nu)}p_{1}^{2}-w_{1}^{FF}p_{\nu)}p\cdot p_{1}-(w_{1}^{FF}+w_{2}^{FF})(p_{\nu)}p_{1}^{2}+p_{1\nu)}p\cdot p_{1})\big)\bigg)\bigg]\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64i}{3}\frac{ip_{\rho}}{3p^{2}}{\epsilon}_{\alpha\sigma(\mu}p_{2}\bigg(\frac{2i}{5}p_{1\nu)}p_{1}^{\sigma}-\frac{3i}{5}p_{2\nu)}p_{1}^{\sigma}-\text{Trace}\bigg)\par\par+(\text{Permutations over $\mu\leftrightarrow\nu\leftrightarrow\rho$})\end{aligned}

(4.35)

And similarly for the QB theory. We thus find that the three-point functions
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}O{\rangle}
of Chern-Simons-matter theories must contain contact terms with specific dependences on the ’t Hooft coupling for the theory to satisfy higher-spin symmetry locally. The function
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})
was determined from perturbative calculations using (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) in
[
15
,
23
]
. In the FF+CS theory, it’s given by
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})\propto{\tilde{\lambda}}
, while for the CB+CS theory it’s given by
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})\propto{\tilde{\lambda}}^{-1}
. The former description implies that the correlator
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}O{\rangle}_{QF}
contains a contact term going like
{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{-1/2}
, while in the latter description the same correlator contains a contact term proportional to
({\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}))^{-1/2}
. Although such terms appear diverging in the
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to\infty,0
limits respectively, they are removed, similarly to
{\langle}JJ{\rangle}_{odd}
.

We find that the two descriptions are inequivalent at the level of contact terms to leading order in large
{\tilde{N}}
. It should be emphasized that still doesn’t necessarily imply that (
4.1
) and (
4.2
) differ in the values of the observable
(\kappa_{1}({\tilde{N}},{\tilde{\lambda}})\mod 1)
, as this value is also dependent on
\mathcal{O}(1/{\tilde{N}})
corrections. Even so, unless the coefficient of
{\langle}JJ{\rangle}_{odd}
in (
4.4
) is integer valued, we find that the contact terms (
4.35
) cannot be naively removed by a different choice of regularization scheme.

In the limits
[
36
,
37
]
, three-point functions
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}O{\rangle}_{QF,QB}
map to corresponding bulk vertices of chiral higher-spin gravity, when written in spinor-helicity variables. Although the
{\tilde{N}}
and
{\tilde{\lambda}}
dependences of various contact terms in (
4.35
) causes these contributions to diverge, we note that we can always add arbitrary contact terms, with the same
{\tilde{N}}
,
{\tilde{\lambda}}
dependence, such that they cancel the Chern-Simons contact terms in the spinor-helicity basis. Thus we conclude that terms (
4.35
) are not visible in the chiral limits
[
36
,
37
]
.

We can also track contact terms appearing in higher correlators through Ward identities of Section
4.2
, and determine if these terms are arbitrary or meaningful. For simplicity, we only consider contributions containing
{\langle}JJ{\rangle}_{odd}
, as the remaining correlators do not contain physically meaningful contact terms. Separating out terms of (
4.20
) leads to:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}|_{CS\;terms}=\\
&\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64}{3}\bigg(p_{1(\mu}+\frac{3}{5}p_{(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\nu)}(-p_{1})J_{\alpha}(p_{1}){\rangle}_{odd}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}\end{aligned}

(4.36)

The right hand side in
p_{2,3}
, however it is scheme-dependent, as it can be subtracted by adding counterterms corresponding to the scalar three-point function. It vanishes in perturbative calculations
[
15
]
of the FF+CS theory. When Legendre transforming the QB theory
[
23
]
, one finds a momentum space constant in
{\langle}OOO{\rangle}_{QF}
, hence the correlator
{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}
contains a contact term going as
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{1/2}
in the CB+CS description. We analyze contact terms appearing in (
4.26
) in similar fashion:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(ip\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}|_{CS\;terms}=\\
&\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{64}{3}\bigg(p_{1(\mu}+\frac{3}{5}p_{(\mu}-\text{Trace}\bigg){\langle}J_{\nu)}(-p_{1})J_{\alpha}(p_{1}){\rangle}_{odd}{\langle}O(p+p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}\\
&+{\tilde{N}}h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\epsilon}_{\alpha\rho(\mu}\bigg({\langle}J_{\nu)}(-p_{2})J_{\beta}(p_{2}){\rangle}_{QF}{\langle}J^{\rho}(-p_{3})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}-{\langle}J_{\nu)}(-p_{2})J_{\beta}(p_{2}){\rangle}_{FF}{\langle}J^{\rho}(-p_{3})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+\begin{Bmatrix}\nu)\leftrightarrow\rho\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}\end{aligned}

(4.37)

We notice that this correlator contains contact terms that are scheme-independent, as removing them in a consistent way would require us to subtract the fractional contact term within the spin-1 two-point function. By expanding the correlators on the right-hand side of (
4.37
) via (
4.4
) and (
4.6
), we find the
{\tilde{\lambda}}
dependence of the contact terms. It would be interesting to try and reproduce this result perturbatively, and furthermore, it would provide constraints on the unfixed function
h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})
.

In principle, one can decide to add various arbitrary contact terms to the theory by adding contributions in dynamical and background fields to the effective action. We may explicitly choose to add terms that break charge conjugation symmetry to a specific correlator. To maintain higher-spin symmetry in such cases, we must add similar terms to correlators related by Ward identities. For example, after adding a contact term to
{\langle}JOO{\rangle}_{QF}
, leads to non-trivial Ward identities for
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})JJO{\rangle}_{QF}
, and so forth.

5
Perturbative Chern-Simons-matter theory calculations

The results of the previous sections can be supplemented by direct computations using the action of QF Chern-Simons-matter theory (
4.1
), at fixed, finite
17
17

17

The line of unitary CFT fixed points exists for
0\leq\lambda\leq 1
.

\lambda=\frac{N}{k}
, while keeping both
N
and
k
large. We set up the computation in momentum space, as in
[
14
,
15
,
41
]
. We employ light-cone coordinates
x^{\pm}=x_{\mp}=(x^{1}\pm ix^{2})/\sqrt{2}
and work in the light-cone gauge
A_{-}=0
. With this choice of gauge fixing, the self-interaction of gauge bosons vanishes, further simplifying perturbative calculations. The gluon propagator is given by:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\langle}A^{a}_{\mu}(-q)A^{b}_{\nu}(p){\rangle}_{QF}=&(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{(3)}(q-p)\delta^{ab}G_{\nu\mu}(p)\\
G_{+3}(p)=&-G_{3+}(p)=\frac{4\pi i}{k}\frac{1}{p^{+}}\end{aligned}

(5.1)

With all other components vanishing. The fermion planar propagator was computed in
[
14
]
, to all orders in
\lambda
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}\psi_{i}(p){\bar{\psi}}^{j}(-q){\rangle}_{QF}=(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{(3)}(p-q)\delta^{j}_{i}S(p)\\
&S(p)=\frac{-i{\gamma}^{\mu}p_{\mu}+i\lambda^{2}{\gamma}^{+}p^{-}+\lambda p_{s}}{p^{2}},\text{ where }p_{s}^{2}=2p^{+}p^{-}\end{aligned}

(5.2)

As in
[
23
,
15
]
, we use cutoff regularization in the radial
p^{+-}
plane, with cutoff
\Lambda
and dimensional regularization along the
p^{3}
direction. We will be working in the collinear limit, so that all external momenta point along the 3-direction, i.e.
p_{i}^{\mu}=p_{i}\delta^{\mu}_{3}
.

Correlators involving spin-0 and spin-1 currents in the QF theory were previously computed to all orders in ’t Hooft coupling in the collinear limit in
[
15
,
41
]
. The computation hinges upon computing the exact vertex functions, by solving the recursive Schwinger-Dyson equations at large
N
. The vertex operators are gauge-dependent and are also computed for external momenta
q
along the 3-axis. We reference those results here:

\displaystyle{\langle}O(-q){\psi}_{i}(k){\bar{\psi}}^{j}(-p){\rangle}_{QF}=

\displaystyle V_{0}(q,p)\delta^{j}_{i}(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{(3)}(q+p-k)

(5.3)

\displaystyle{\langle}J^{\pm}(-q){\psi}_{i}(k){\bar{\psi}}^{j}(-p){\rangle}_{QF}=

\displaystyle V^{\pm}(q,p)\delta^{j}_{i}(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{(3)}(q+p-k)

(5.4)

As a shorthand, the vertex functions are written in terms of dimensionless variables. The loop momentum appears as
y=\frac{2p_{s}}{|q_{3}|}
, the cutoff in the
p^{+-}
plane is given by
\Lambda^{\prime}=\frac{2\Lambda}{|q_{3}|}
and
\hat{\lambda}=\lambda\text{sign}(q_{3})
controls the coupling to the gauge field
18
18

18

Note that
\hat{\lambda}
is invariant under a parity transformation combined with
\lambda\to-\lambda
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} V_{0}(q,p)=&\frac{2\lambda p_{+}}{p_{s}}\frac{1-iy\hat{\lambda}-(1+iy\hat{\lambda})e^{-2i\hat{\lambda}\arctan(y)}}{\hat{\lambda}y(1+e^{-2i\hat{\lambda}\arctan(\Lambda^{\prime})})}{\gamma}^{+}+\frac{1+e^{-2i\hat{\lambda}\arctan(y)}}{1+e^{-2i\hat{\lambda}\arctan(\Lambda^{\prime})}}\mathds{1}\\
V^{+}(q,p)=&\frac{i}{2}(1-iy\hat{\lambda}+(1+iy\hat{\lambda})e^{2i\hat{\lambda}(\arctan(\Lambda^{\prime})-\arctan(y))}){\gamma}^{+}+\frac{ip^{+}}{q_{3}}(1-e^{2i\hat{\lambda}(\arctan(\Lambda^{\prime})-\arctan(y))})\mathds{1}\\
V^{-}(q,p)=&i{\gamma}^{-}+\frac{i(p^{-})^{2}}{p_{s}^{2}y^{2}}((1+iy\hat{\lambda})e^{-2i\hat{\lambda}\arctan(y))}-(1-iy\hat{\lambda})){\gamma}^{+}+\frac{ip^{-}}{q_{3}y^{2}}(1-2iy\hat{\lambda}-e^{-2i\hat{\lambda}\arctan(y)})\mathds{1}\end{aligned}

(5.5)

In Appendix
C.4
, we compute the vertex corresponding to the plus-component of a general spin-
s
current:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}J_{s}^{\pm}(-q){\psi}_{i}(k){\bar{\psi}}^{j}(-p){\rangle}_{QF}=V_{s}^{++...+}(q,p)\delta^{j}_{i}(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{(3)}(q+p-k)\\[3.0pt]
&V_{s}^{++...+}(q,p)=\beta_{s}(p^{+})^{s-1}V^{+}(q,p)\end{aligned}

(5.6)

Where
\beta_{s}
is just an overall normalization factor
19
19

19

In our conventions (
1.4
) we have
\beta_{2}=1
and
\beta_{3}=8i/3
.
. We now proceed to compute three- and four-point functions of the QF theory, involving the stress-energy tensor and spin-3 current.

5.1
{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{QF}
calculation

Using the vertex expressions of the previous section we can evaluate correlation functions of QF Chern-Simons-matter theory. We first compute the three-point function
{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}
for momenta
q_{i}
pointing along the 3-axis. The one-loop contributions to the parity-odd structure of this correlator were analyzed in
[
14
]
. At large
N
, the contributing diagrams are displayed in Figure
5
.

Figure 5
:
Diagrams contributing to the three-point function
{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}
. The exact spin-2 vertex is represented by an encircled cross, while the exact spin-1 vertices are represented by crossed squares. The exact fermion propagators are represented by filled circles.

The expression reads:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}=\\[5.0pt]
&-N\int\frac{d^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}Tr\bigg[S(p+q_{1})V^{++}(q_{1},p)S(p)V^{-}(q_{2},p)S(p-q_{2})V^{-}(q_{3},p)\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad+S(p+q_{1})V^{++}(q_{1},p)S(p)V^{-}(q_{3},p)S(p-q_{3})V^{-}(q_{2},p)\bigg]\end{aligned}

(5.7)

Using the vertex expressions (
5.4
), (
5.6
) and the fermion propagator (
5.2
) the integral can be evaluated explicitly. In the collinear limit, momentum conservation implies
q_{1}=-q_{2}-q_{3}
. After regularizing, we take the
p^{+-}
plane cutoff
\Lambda
to infinity, recovering a gauge-invariant expression for the correlator. Defining
{\hat{\lambda}}_{i}=\lambda\text{sign}(q_{i})
, for
i=1,2,3
, we have:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}=&\frac{iN}{32\pi\lambda}\frac{q_{2}q_{3}(2q_{2}^{2}+2q_{3}^{2}+5q_{2}q_{3})-q_{2}^{2}q_{3}^{2}e^{-i\pi({\hat{\lambda}}_{2}+{\hat{\lambda}}_{3})}}{q_{2}q_{3}(q_{2}+q_{3})}\\
&+\frac{iN}{32\pi\lambda}\frac{q_{2}^{2}(q_{2}+q_{3})^{2}e^{-i\pi{\hat{\lambda}}_{2}}+q_{3}^{2}(q_{2}+q_{3})^{2}e^{-i\pi{\hat{\lambda}}_{3}}-(q_{2}+q_{3})^{4}e^{i\pi{\hat{\lambda}}_{1}}}{q_{2}q_{3}(q_{2}+q_{3})}\end{aligned}

(5.8)

The result is
q_{2}\leftrightarrow q_{3}
symmetric, as expected since the currents are bosonic operators. To rewrite the correlator in standard form, we expand the exponentials and use the relations between parameters of the theory and higher-spin variables
{\tilde{\lambda}}
,
{\tilde{N}}
(
4.3
). We find:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}=\\
&\quad\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\bigg(\frac{q_{2}^{3}(q_{2}+2q_{3}){\text{sign}}(q_{2})+q_{3}^{3}(2q_{2}+q_{3}){\text{sign}}(q_{3})-(q_{2}+q_{3})^{4}{\text{sign}}(q_{2}+q_{3})}{64q_{2}q_{3}(q_{2}+q_{3})}\bigg)\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\bigg(i\frac{q_{2}q_{3}(1+{\text{sign}}(q_{2}){\text{sign}}(q_{3}))}{32(q_{2}+q_{3})}\bigg)\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\bigg(\frac{(q_{2}+q_{3})^{2}(q_{2}^{2}{\text{sign}}(q_{2})+q_{3}^{2}{\text{sign}}(q_{3}))+q_{2}^{2}q_{3}^{2}({\text{sign}}(q_{2})+{\text{sign}}(q_{3}))-(q_{2}+q_{3})^{4}{\text{sign}}(q_{2}+q_{3})}{64q_{2}q_{3}(q_{2}+q_{3})}\bigg)\\
&+{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\bigg(\frac{i}{32}(q_{2}+q_{3})\bigg)\end{aligned}

(5.9)

The correlator satisfies the form (
4.6
) predicted by weakly broken higher-spin symmetry. One can check that the three non-local terms in (
5.9
) satisfy an epsilon-transform relation in the collinear limit:

\displaystyle{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{odd}=-i{\text{sign}}(q_{2}+q_{3}){\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{FF-CB}

(5.10)

This relation is just the collinear limit version of:

\displaystyle{\langle}T_{\mu\nu}(-q_{1})J_{\alpha}(-q_{2})J_{\beta}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{odd}=-{\langle}({\epsilon}\cdot T)_{\mu\nu}(-q_{1})J_{\alpha}(-q_{2})J_{\beta}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{FF-CB}

(5.11)

The perturbative result implies that the epsilon-transform holds only with respect to
T_{\mu\nu}
, and not with respect to the vector currents. These results further corroborate that the parity-odd structure can be represented in terms of correlators of the free and critical theories. Equations (
5.10
) and (
5.11
) are consistent with the relation observed in
[
39
]
, obtained by analyzing higher-spin Ward identities of the spin-4 pseudocharge. Due to this property, it was argued in
[
36
,
37
]
that in specific limits, the three-point function
{\langle}TJJ{\rangle}_{QF}
only contains components with a specific sign of overall helicity, when written in spinor-helicity variables.

The correlator
{\langle}TJJ{\rangle}_{QF}
, computed in the FF+CS description, contains an additional parity-odd contact term which appears to diverge in the limit
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to\infty
, however it is removed in the critical-bosonic theory due to parity. Given that our regularization scheme breaks conformal invariance, we should emphasize that the contact term in (
5.9
) is not generally expected to be conformally-invariant. One may try to relate this term to the
{\langle}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
parity-breaking contact term through the
{\langle}(\partial\cdot T)_{\nu}J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}{\rangle}_{QF}
translation Ward identity, however its kinematic dependence suggests that it is a distinct contribution. This is consistent with the observations of
[
43
]
. For general momenta and Lorentz indices, the term in (
5.9
) could take the form:

\displaystyle{\langle}T_{\mu\nu}(-q_{1})J_{\alpha}(-q_{2})J_{\beta}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{\text{contact term}}=\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{64}g_{\alpha(\mu}{\epsilon}_{\nu)\beta\rho}q_{1}^{\rho}+(\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta)

(5.12)

This term is similar to the Chern-Simons contact term appearing in the two-point function of the spin-1 current. If we we denote the fields sourcing the stress tensor
T_{\mu\nu}
and vector current
J_{\alpha}
by
\mathcal{A}_{2}^{\mu\nu}
and
\mathcal{A}_{1}^{\alpha}
, respectively, this contact term corresponds to the appearance of
q_{1}^{\rho}\mathcal{A}_{2}^{\sigma_{1}\sigma_{2}}(q_{1}){\epsilon}_{\rho\sigma_{2}\sigma_{3}}\mathcal{A}_{1\sigma_{1}}(q_{2})\mathcal{A}_{1}^{\sigma_{3}}(q_{3})+(\sigma_{1}\leftrightarrow\sigma_{3})
in the effective action of the theory. It would be interesting to further analyze the effects of such terms in the quasifermionic theory. Although the term (
5.12
) diverges in the chiral limits discussed in
[
36
,
37
]
, we note that one can add an arbitrary contact term with the same
{\tilde{N}}
,
{\tilde{\lambda}}
dependence, so that the contact terms cancel when written in spinor-helicity variables. Therefore, we argue that the term (
5.12
) does not contribute in the chiral limit.

Finally, it is interesting to check the three-dimensional bosonization duality between QB and QF Chern-Simons-matter theories by comparing the correlator (
5.9
) to the expression for
{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QB}
obtained in
[
23
]
. The bosonic theory discussed therein has slightly broken higher-spin symmetry described by analogous variables
{\tilde{N}}_{QB},{\tilde{\lambda}}_{QB}
. The precise relation of these variables to the quasifermionic parameters used in this text was found in
[
15
]
to be
{\tilde{N}}_{QB}={\tilde{N}}
and
{\tilde{\lambda}}_{QB}=-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{-1}
. Correlators of the QB theory can be decomposed in terms of
{\tilde{N}}_{QB}
and
{\tilde{\lambda}}_{QB}
analogously
20
20

20

The
{\tilde{\lambda}}_{QB}\to 0
limit corresponds to the free boson (FB) theory, while the
{\tilde{\lambda}}_{QB}\to\infty
limit corresponds to the critical fermion (CF) theory.
to (
4.6
). Expressing
{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{QB}
via
{\tilde{N}}
and
{\tilde{\lambda}}
, we find:

\displaystyle{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{QB}=\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{CF}-\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{QB,odd}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{FB}

(5.13)

Here we denote the odd structure appearing in the QB theory by
{\langle}TJJ{\rangle}_{QB,odd}
, to emphasize its difference to the odd structure
{\langle}TJJ{\rangle}_{odd}={\langle}TJJ{\rangle}_{QF,odd}
appearing in the QF theory, in (
5.9
). The QB and QF theories are related by a Legendre transformation with respect to the scalar current. At large
N
, this does not affect correlators containing only currents with spin
s\geq 1
. Therefore, we expect the non-contact terms in (
5.9
) and (
5.13
) to coincide. Comparing to the results of
[
23
]
, we find:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{CF}={\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}-\frac{|q_{2}|}{32}-\frac{|q_{3}|}{32}\\
{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}={\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}-\frac{|q_{2}|}{32}-\frac{|q_{3}|}{32}\\
{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QB,odd}=-{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF,odd}\end{aligned}

(5.14)

Which is consistent with the bosonization duality. In addition, we found that the QF correlator (
5.9
) contains a contact term going as
{\tilde{\lambda}}
while
{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{QB}
was found to have contact terms appearing with different odd functions of the quasibosonic ’t Hooft coupling
\lambda_{QB}
. The difference in contact term coefficients reflects computations in different descriptions of the QF theory.

5.2
{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{QF}
calculation

Higher correlators in theories with weakly broken higher-spin symmetry have been a topic of interesting research. Computations of four-point functions using bootstrap arguments were carried out in
[
44
,
45
,
46
]
. Furthermore, computations of scalar
n
-point functions were outlined in
[
47
]
. To all orders in perturbation theory, four-point functions of spinning operators were determined in
[
41
]
. We follow the procedure established therein to compute the four-point function
{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{QF}
in the collinear limit, first giving a brief overview of the computation.

Figure 6
:
Diagrams contributing to
{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3})O(-q_{4}){\rangle}_{QF}
: (a) Box-type diagram (b) Ladder-type diagram. The exact vertices of the spin-0,1,2 currents are represented by encircled crosses, crossed squares and blank squares respectively. (c) The four-fermion vertex is obtained by summing all ladders made from gluon lines connected by exact fermion propagators.

The diagrams contributing at finite
\lambda
can be split into two types: Box-type diagrams (Figure
6
(a)) and Ladder-type diagrams (Figure
6
(b)). The former are diagrams where non-adjacent fermion propagators are not connected. The latter involve the effective four-fermion vertex, as displayed in Figure
6
(c), linking non-adjacent fermion lines. We write the vertex as
\Gamma^{jn}_{im}(k,q,p)=\Gamma_{AB}(k,q,p)(\gamma_{A})^{n}_{m}(\gamma_{B})^{j}_{i}
, for which the full expression can be found in
[
41
]
. At leading order in large
N
, these are the only contributions to the four-point function. Accounting for all permutations of current insertions, there are 6 different Box diagrams and 12 different Ladder diagrams.

We denote the vertex functions as in (
5.5
) and (
5.6
), but suppress the loop momentum variables. In the collinear limit, momentum conservation implies
q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3}+q_{4}=0
. The box-type diagram in Figure
6
(a) evaluates to:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{Box}(p)=-N\int\frac{d^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}Tr\bigg[&V^{++}(q_{1})S(p)V_{0}(-q_{4})S(p+q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})\\
&\cdot V^{-}(q_{3})S(p+q_{1}+q_{2})V^{-}(q_{2})S(p+q_{1})\bigg]\end{aligned}

(5.15)

And similarly, the ladder-type diagram in Figure
6
(b) is expressed as:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \text{Ladder}(p,k)=-N\int\frac{d^{3}p\;d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{6}}Tr\bigg[&S(k+q_{1}+q_{2})V^{-}(q_{2})S(k+q_{1})V^{++}(q_{1})S(k){\gamma}^{A}\\
&\cdot S(p)V_{0}(q_{4})S(p-q_{4})V^{-}(q_{3})S(p-q_{3}-q_{4}){\gamma}^{B}\bigg]\Gamma_{AB}(k,q_{1}+q_{2},p)\end{aligned}

(5.16)

The integrals over loop momenta are now performed in specific order, to simplify the intermediate steps as much as possible. We first perform integrations along the 3-axis, by integrating over
dp_{3}
and
dk_{3}
. The angular integrals
d\theta_{p}
, and
d\theta_{k}
are performed by computing residues in the complex plane. The planar integral over
dk_{s}
can now be performed without cutoffs in the
k^{+-}
plane.

In the final step we sum up all the integrands, which leverages any exchange symmetries present in the correlator. Beforehand, one can check that the separate integrals do not diverge by themselves. Schematically, we write the final step as:

\displaystyle{\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3})O(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}=-N\int_{0}^{\Lambda}dp_{s}\sum_{\text{
\begin{tabular}[t]{@{}c@{}}Insertion\\
permutations\end{tabular}}}\bigg(\text{Box}(p_{s})+\text{Ladder}(p_{s})\bigg)

By sending
\Lambda\to\infty
, we recover the correlator in terms of
N
and
\lambda
. When rewriting the expression in terms of higher-spin symmetry variables
{\tilde{N}}
and
{\tilde{\lambda}}
it is important to note that the scalar vertex (
5.3
) computed in
[
15
]
is normalized so that the scalar two point function scales as
