{\langle}OO{\rangle}_{QF}={\tilde{N}}(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}){\langle}OO{\rangle}_{FF}
. In our normalization, the final result can be expressed as (suppresing the momentum arguments for brevity):

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\langle}T_{--}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3})O(-q_{4}){\rangle}_{QF}=&\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}^{3}}{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}^{3}}{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{X_{1}}\\
&+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}^{3}}{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{X_{2}}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{3}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}^{3}}{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{CB}\end{aligned}

(5.19)

The full expression for general values of collinear
q_{1,2,3}
is given in Appendix
A
. As expected, the result is
q_{2}\leftrightarrow q_{3}
symmetric. In addition to free/critical theory results, we find two additional structures. For general signs of momenta, we do not find any relations between the four structures of
{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{QF}
and they appear to be distinct, which is inconsistent with the form of
{\langle}J_{s_{1}}J_{s_{2}}J_{s_{3}}O{\rangle}_{QF}
conjectured in
[
38
]
. It was pointed out
21
21

21

We thank Sachin Jain and Dhruva Sathyanarayanan for this remark.
that, when the momenta of the vector currents are of the same sign, and opposite the sign of the scalar and stress tensor, the four structures of
{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{QF}
reduce to two structures, up to contact terms. It would be interesting to understand if this property holds more generally, beyond the collinear limit.

5.3
{\langle}J_{---}J_{+}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{QF}
calculation

The computation of
{\langle}J_{---}J_{+}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{QF}
closely follows the steps of the previous subsection. The contributing diagrams are again displayed in Figure
6
, however the scalar vertex is replaced by the spin-3 vertex using (
5.6
) and the stress tensor vertex is replaced by a third vector vertex (
5.5
):

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\text{Box}(p)=-N\int\frac{d^{3}p}{(2\pi)^{3}}Tr\bigg[V^{-}(q_{1})S(p)V^{+++}(-q_{4})S(p+q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot V^{-}(q_{3})S(p+q_{1}+q_{2})V^{-}(q_{2})S(p+q_{1})\bigg]\\
&\text{Ladder}(p,k)=-N\int\frac{d^{3}p\;d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{6}}Tr\bigg[S(k+q_{1}+q_{2})V^{-}(q_{2})S(k+q_{1})V^{-}(q_{1})S(k){\gamma}^{A}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot S(p)V^{+++}(q_{4})S(p-q_{4})V^{-}(q_{3})S(p-q_{3}-q_{4}){\gamma}^{B}\bigg]\Gamma_{AB}(k,q_{1}+q_{2},p)\end{aligned}

(5.20)

And similarly for other diagrams which involve permutations of vertices. An additional subtlety arises as extra factors of
p^{\pm}
in the denominator cause the integral to grow linearly with the cutoff
\Lambda
in the
p^{+-}
plane. Similarly to
[
41
]
, one can check numerically that for general values of
q_{i},\Lambda
the separate integrals converge, hence we may sum the integrands before performing the final
dp_{s}
integration. We find:

\displaystyle{\langle}J_{---}(q_{1}+q_{2}+q_{3})J_{+}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}\propto\par f\bigg(e^{2\pi i\lambda\arctan(\frac{2\Lambda}{P})}\bigg)+\frac{i\Lambda}{2\pi}

(5.21)

Where
P
is a shorthand for different linear combinations of
q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}
. We may now take the
\Lambda\to\infty
limit. Upon doing this, the first term on the right hand side of (
5.21
) converges, while the linear divergence can be regularized by introducing a local counterterm. The final result can be written in terms of
{\tilde{N}},{\tilde{\lambda}}
as (
q_{4}=-q_{1}-q_{2}-q_{3}
; again suppressing momentum arguments):

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}J_{---}(-q_{4})J_{+}(-q_{1})J_{+}(-q_{2})J_{+}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}=\\[5.0pt]
&\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}{\langle}J_{---}J_{+}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}{\langle}J_{---}J_{+}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{X_{1}}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}{\langle}J_{---}J_{+}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{X_{2}}\\[5.0pt]
&+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{3}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}{\langle}J_{---}J_{+}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{X_{3}}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{4}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}{\langle}J_{---}J_{+}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{CB}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{5}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}\bigg(\frac{q_{4}}{6}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(5.22)

In addition to
FF
and
CB
structures, we find three additional pieces
X_{1,2,3}
which vanish in both the free and critical limits. The full expression is given in Appendix
B
. The
{\tilde{\lambda}}
dependence of non-contact terms in (
5.22
) nicely matches with the higher-spin Ward identity (
4.27
). The last term on the right hand side of (
5.22
) naively diverges as
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to\infty
, however this should be understood as a contact term. Outside the collinear limit, we may guess the form of this term
22
22

22

Similarly to the
{\langle}TJJ{\rangle}_{QF}
case, we do not necessarily expect this term to be conformally invariant, as the regularization scheme we employ breaks conformal invariance.
, consistent with Lorentz covariance to be:

\displaystyle{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}(-q_{4})J_{\alpha}(-q_{1})J_{\beta}(-q_{2})J_{\gamma}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{\text{contact}}\propto\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{5}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}g_{\alpha\mu}g_{\beta\nu}{\epsilon}_{\sigma\gamma\rho}q_{4}^{\sigma}+\text{ index permutations}

(5.23)

It would be natural to interpret this contact term as stemming from a slightly broken higher-spin Ward identity, and indeed there are various possibilities on how such a term may arise. For example, one may consider the action of the spin-3 pseudocharge on
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}J_{1}O{\rangle}_{QF}
and similar Ward identities involving currents of spin
s>3
. We will leave a comprehensive study of these four-point function contact terms to future work.

Here, we note that the
{\tilde{\lambda}}
dependence of the contact term (
5.23
), obtained through perturbation theory, matches the expectation (
4.37
) from equating local terms of the
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
Ward identity, in the FF+CS description, i.e. when
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})\propto{\tilde{\lambda}}
. Although this is not surprising, it is not precisely clear if the contact term appearing in (
5.22
) is apriori the same as the contact term in (
4.37
), since
{\langle}J_{---}J_{+}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{QF}
drops out of the Ward identity for collinear momenta along the 3-axis. It would be interesting to explore this further by computing the same correlator in the CB+CS picture, and ultimately checking if the computed terms come from Chern-Simons background terms.

The end result is written in Appendix
B
in terms of
p_{i}=-q_{i}
. It satisfies the expected exchange symmetry
p_{1}\leftrightarrow p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}
. Interestingly, the five non-local structures also satisfy
23
23

23

We use the shorthand
{\langle}...{\rangle}_{A\pm B}={\langle}...{\rangle}_{A}\pm{\langle}...{\rangle}_{B}
:

\displaystyle{\langle}J_{---}(p_{4})J_{+}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{+}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF+CB-X_{2}}=i{\text{sign}}(p_{4})\bigg({\langle}J_{---}(p_{4})J_{+}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{+}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{X_{1}-X_{3}}+\frac{p_{4}}{6}\bigg)

(5.24)

This relation appears as an epsilon-transform with respect to the spin-3 current in the collinear limit. In addition to the Ward identity analysis of
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
(see discussion after (
4.27
)), this strongly supports the claim that the general four-point function
{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}J_{\gamma}{\rangle}_{QF}
satisfies the following epsilon-transform relation up to contact terms:

\displaystyle{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF+CB-X_{2}}={\langle}({\epsilon}\cdot J)_{\mu\nu\rho}(p)J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{X_{1}-X_{3}}+\text{contact terms}

(5.25)

Ultimately, a full check on this identity would require a direct calculation of the four-point function for general momenta and Lorentz indices, or possibly a different approach altogether, which we leave as a topic of future work.

5.4
Checks of three- and four-point function Ward identities

The perturbative results of the previous sections can be used to explicitly check higher-spin Ward identities. Such non-trivial checks provide further verification of the higher-spin algebra. However, in the collinear limit, identifying contact terms is not always obvious, which makes the equations difficult to track. Furthermore, for certain checks we require correlators with specific Lorentz indices, which can be difficult to compute in the full Chern-Simons-matter theory. We thus confine ourselves to verifying relations between correlators of free theories. When borrowing results from
[
15
,
23
,
41
]
, we remind the reader that our FF (FB) correlators are normalized as
\frac{1}{2}
those of a theory of a single Dirac fermion (complex boson)
24
24

24

In particular, the results for
{\langle}J_{+}J_{-}OO{\rangle}
and
{\langle}J_{+}J_{-}J_{+}J_{-}{\rangle}
in
[
41
]
are corrected by a factor of
-\frac{1}{2}
, while the result for
{\langle}OOOO{\rangle}
is corrected by a minus sign. This can be checked by first taking the
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0
limit and then performing the calculation
. This corresponds to our
{\tilde{N}}\to 1,{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0
and
{\tilde{N}}\to 1,{\tilde{\lambda}}\to\infty
limits, with the latter involving additional Legendre transformation discussed in Section
3
, to reach the critical fixed point.

\underline{\textbf{Ward identities of three-point functions}}

We will first discuss the
{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}OO]{\rangle}_{FF,FB}
Ward identities using the results of Appendix
C
. The FF Ward identity reads:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &-a_{1}{\epsilon}_{\sigma\alpha(\mu}p_{1\nu)}p_{1}^{\sigma}{\langle}O(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}+ia_{2}p_{1\mu}{\langle}T_{\nu)\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}\\
&-a_{0}{\epsilon}_{\rho\sigma(\mu}\bigg(p_{2\nu)}p_{2}^{\rho}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J^{\sigma}(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}+\begin{Bmatrix}2\leftrightarrow 3\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)=0\end{aligned}

(5.26)

The equation holds up to contact terms. The correlator
{\langle}OOO{\rangle}_{FF}
, vanishes, as the scalar current is parity odd. Using (
C.21
) and (
C.26
), we find that the right-hand side doesn’t contain contact terms if and only if
a_{2}=2ia_{0}
, which is consistent with the relations found in Section
2.2
. After using this relation, we find that the left-hand side of (
5.26
) becomes a sum of contact terms:

\displaystyle{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3})]{\rangle}_{FF}|_{\text{contact terms}}=\frac{a_{0}}{16}p_{1\alpha}p_{1\mu}p_{1\nu}+\bigg(\frac{1}{48}p_{2(\mu}\delta_{\nu)\alpha}(3p_{1}+2p_{2}-4p_{3})+\begin{Bmatrix}2\leftrightarrow 3\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)

(5.27)

Analogously, we check the
{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3})]{\rangle}_{FB}
Ward identity. This serves to confirm the FB algebra discussed in Section
2.3
, but also the associated algebra of the critical boson theory. The Ward identity was stated in (
C.8
).

The expression for
{\langle}OOO{\rangle}_{FB}
can be obtained from a 1-loop calculation, and in our normalization reads
{\langle}O(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}=64(p_{1}p_{2}p_{3})^{-1}
. The remaining correlators were computed similarly and are given by (
C.22
) and (
C.27
). Inserting these results into (
C.8
), we find that the non-contact terms on the left-hand side cancel if and only if the coefficients
b_{0,1,2,3}
obey
b_{1}=-\frac{b_{0}}{128}
,
b_{2}=\frac{b_{0}}{128}
,
b_{3}=\frac{b_{0}}{4}
. This is consistent with the results of Section
2.3
. Given these constraints, the left-hand side simplifies to a sum of contact terms:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3})]{\rangle}_{FB}|_{\text{contact terms}}=-\frac{2ib_{0}p_{1(\mu}\delta_{\nu)\alpha}}{3}\bigg(\frac{1}{p_{2}}+\frac{1}{p_{3}}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(5.28)

We discuss the remaining Ward identities in the collinear limit, with all momenta pointing along the
3
-axis.

The
{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}J_{\gamma}]{\rangle}_{FF}
Ward identity can be written as:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &-a_{1}{\epsilon}_{\sigma\alpha(\mu}p_{1\nu)}p_{1}^{\sigma}{\langle}O(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}+ia_{2}p_{1(\mu}{\langle}T_{\nu)\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}\\[5.0pt]
&+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}=0\end{aligned}

(5.29)

Choosing
p_{i}^{\mu}=p_{i}\delta_{3}^{\mu}
, and
\mu=3
,
\alpha=\beta=+
,
\gamma=\nu=-
, the equation becomes
25
25

25

Note that the coordinate system is right-handed, i.e.
{\epsilon}_{+-3}=i
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &-\frac{ia_{1}}{2}p_{1}^{2}{\langle}O(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}-\frac{ia_{1}}{2}p_{2}^{2}{\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})O(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{ia_{2}}{2}p_{3}{\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})T_{--}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}\\
&+\frac{ia_{2}}{2}p_{1}{\langle}T_{+-}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{ia_{2}}{2}p_{1}{\langle}T_{+-}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}=0\end{aligned}

(5.30)

Momentum conservation fixes
p_{3}=-p_{1}-p_{2}
. The correlator
{\langle}T_{+-}J_{+}J_{-}{\rangle}
is not determined by the preceeding analysis, however, one can find it analogous to (
5.7
), using the free fermion vertices
V^{+-}(q,p)=ip^{(+}{\gamma}^{-)}
and
V^{\pm}(q,p)=i{\gamma}^{\pm}
and taking the limit
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0
. The final result is:

\displaystyle{\langle}T_{+-}(-p_{2}-p_{3})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}=-\frac{p_{2}^{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{2})+p_{3}^{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{3})}{64(p_{2}+p_{3})}

(5.31)

Using (
5.9
), (
5.31
) as well as the results of
[
15
]
, the Ward identity (
5.30
) can be checked in the collinear limit. We find:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(4a_{1}-ia_{2})\bigg(-\frac{p_{1}^{4}{\text{sign}}(p_{3})}{64p_{2}p_{3}}-\frac{p_{1}^{4}{\text{sign}}(p_{1})}{64p_{2}p_{3}}-\frac{p_{1}^{3}{\text{sign}}(p_{1})}{32p_{3}}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\\
&+\frac{p_{1}p_{2}}{64p_{3}}\bigg({\text{sign}}(p_{1})(4a_{1}p_{2}+ia_{2}p_{1})+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\\
&-\frac{1}{128}{\text{sign}}(p_{3})\bigg(p_{3}^{2}(4a_{1}+ia_{2})-5(4a_{1}-ia_{2})\left(p_{1}^{2}+p_{2}^{2}\right)\bigg)=0\end{aligned}

(5.32)

The equation should hold up to contact terms, which can be removed by a suitable regularization scheme. It is clear that the only choice that makes all the non-contact terms on the right-hand side vanish is
a_{2}=-4ia_{1}
. This is consistent with the constraints obtained in Section
2
. After inserting this relation, equation (
5.32
) simplifies, and we are left with:

\displaystyle{\langle}[Q_{3-},J_{+}J_{+}J_{-}]{\rangle}_{FF}|_{\text{contact terms}}=-\frac{a_{1}}{16}p_{1}{\text{sign}}(p_{1})p_{2}-\frac{a_{1}}{16}p_{1}p_{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{2})+\frac{a_{1}}{16}p_{3}^{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{3})

(5.33)

We argue that these terms are contact terms, as they all are polynomials in two of the three momenta. Therefore, we find that the correlators satisfy the higher-spin Ward identity, with the expected algebra coefficients.

\underline{\textbf{Ward identities of four-point functions}}

Next, we verify the
{\langle}[Q_{3},J_{1}OOO]{\rangle}_{FF}
and
{\langle}[Q_{3},J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}O]{\rangle}_{FF}
Ward identities in the collinear limit. We will make use of the results for
{\langle}OOOO{\rangle}_{FF}
,
{\langle}J_{+}J_{-}OO{\rangle}_{FF}
and
{\langle}J_{+}J_{+}J_{-}J_{-}{\rangle}_{FF}
found in
[
41
]
and our result of
{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{FF}
as in (
A.1
). Additionally, we will need correlators involving the
T_{+-}
vertex. We only need the FF expressions, which can be obtained by combining free theory vertex
V^{+-}(q,p)=ip^{(+}{\gamma}^{-)}
with expressions (
5.2
), (
5.3
), (
5.4
) in the
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0
limit. Each correlator is obtained by summing 6 box diagrams, given by (
5.15
). We find that
{\langle}T_{+-}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}
vanishes, while:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\langle}T_{+-}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})O(-p_{1}-p_{2}-p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}=\frac{i}{32p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\par\bigg(\frac{p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2})^{3}}{|p_{1}+p_{2}|}-\frac{p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{3})^{3}}{|p_{1}+p_{3}|}-\frac{p_{2}^{4}}{|p_{2}|}+\frac{p_{3}^{4}}{|p_{3}|}\\
\qquad\qquad\qquad-\frac{(p_{2}-p_{3})\left(|p_{2}+p_{3}|(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{3}-(p_{2}+p_{3})^{3}|p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}|\right)}{|p_{2}+p_{3}||p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}|}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(5.34)

As a consistency check on this result, we can confirm that it aligns with the translation Ward identity:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}(\partial\cdot T)_{\nu}(p_{1})J_{\alpha}(p_{2})J_{\beta}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}=\delta(x_{1}-x){\langle}\partial_{\nu}J_{\alpha}(x)J_{\beta}(x_{2})O(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}\\
&+\delta(x_{2}-x){\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})\partial_{\nu}J_{\beta}(x)O(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}+\delta(x_{3}-x){\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})J_{\beta}(x_{2})\partial_{\nu}O(x){\rangle}_{FF}\end{aligned}

(5.35)

Tracelessness of the stress-tensor implies
T_{33}+2T_{+-}=0
. As all momenta point along the 3-axis, we have:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} ip_{1}{\langle}T_{+-}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}=&-\frac{1}{2}\bigg(i(p_{1}+p_{2}){\langle}J_{+}(p_{1}+p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}\\
&\qquad\quad+i(p_{1}+p_{3}){\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})J_{-}(p_{1}+p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}\\
&\qquad\quad-i(p_{2}+p_{3}){\langle}J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})O(p_{1}+p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(5.36)

Where
p_{4}=-p_{1}-p_{2}-p_{3}
. Borrowing the result for
{\langle}J_{+}J_{-}O{\rangle}_{FF}
from
[
15
]
, we verify that this relation indeed holds.

We first check the
{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}OOO]{\rangle}_{FF}
higher-spin Ward identity. The equation reads:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &-a_{1}{\epsilon}_{\sigma\alpha(\mu}p_{1\nu)}p_{1}^{\sigma}{\langle}O(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}+ic_{2}p_{1(\mu}{\langle}T_{\nu)\alpha}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}\\
&-a_{0}\bigg({\epsilon}_{\sigma\rho(\mu}p_{2\nu)}p_{2}^{\sigma}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\rho}(p_{2})O(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}+\{2\leftrightarrow 3\}+\{2\leftrightarrow 4\}\bigg)=0\end{aligned}

(5.37)

In the collinear limit, with
\mu=3
,
\nu=-
,
\alpha=+
, we find:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &-\frac{ia_{1}}{2}p_{1}^{2}{\langle}O(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}+\cancel{\frac{ia_{2}}{2}p_{1}{\langle}T_{+-}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}}\\
&-\frac{ia_{0}}{2}\bigg(p_{2}^{2}{\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})J_{-}(p_{2})O(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}+\{2\leftrightarrow 3\}+\{2\leftrightarrow 4\}\bigg)=0\par\end{aligned}

(5.38)

Momentum conservation implies
p_{4}=-p_{1}-p_{2}-p_{3}
, therefore we will discuss the equation in terms of
p_{1,2,3}
. The Ward identities can be discussed for different values of momenta. A way to check all cases is to write out (
5.38
) for all possible values of
\{{\text{sign}}(p_{1}),{\text{sign}}(p_{2}),{\text{sign}}(p_{3}),{\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{2}),{\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{3}),{\text{sign}}(p_{2}+p_{3}),{\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})\}
. Excluding contradictory assumptions, there are 32 possible regimes. We will analyze the equation for
p_{1}>0,p_{2}>0,p_{3}>0
and leave to the reader to check that the same conclusions follow in all cases. The terms of (
5.38
) become:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &p_{1}^{2}{\langle}O(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}=\frac{p_{1}^{2}(p_{1}p_{2}+p_{2}p_{3}+p_{3}p_{1})}{2(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{2}+p_{3})(p_{3}+p_{1})}\\
&p_{2}^{2}{\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})J_{-}(p_{2})O(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}=\frac{p_{2}^{2}}{4p_{4}}+\frac{p_{1}p_{2}^{3}}{4(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{2}+p_{3})(p_{3}+p_{1})}\par\end{aligned}

(5.39)

Inserting
a_{0}=-2a_{1}
and (
5.39
) into (
5.38
), we find that the equation is satisfied up to contact terms. Furthermore, it is easy to verify that any other choice of constants
a_{0,1}
results in terms that are not polynomials in three of the four momenta, and thus cannot be interpreted as contact terms. Therefore,
a_{0}=-2a_{1}
is the unique constraint obtained from the Ward identity and is consistent with the FF algebra (
2.17
). The remaining terms in (
5.38
) after enforcing the constraint are:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\langle}[Q_{3-},J_{+}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3})O(p_{4})]{\rangle}_{FF}|_{\text{contact terms}}=\frac{ia_{1}p_{1}^{2}}{4p_{4}}-\frac{ia_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}{2p_{4}}\end{aligned}

(5.40)

These would need to be absorbed into some of the correlators involved, for the Ward identity to be consistent up to contact terms. Different expressions for (
5.40
) are obtained in different kinematic regimes, however we argue that these should come from contact terms of the covariant equation, since all the terms appearing are polynomials in at least two of the four momenta. It would be interesting to reconstruct these contact terms, by comparing the results with general momentum expressions.

Next, we check the Ward identity
{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}J_{\gamma}O]{\rangle}_{FF}
. The equation reads:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\bigg(-a_{1}{\epsilon}_{\sigma\alpha(\mu}p_{1\nu)}p_{1}^{\sigma}{\langle}O(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}+ia_{2}p_{1(\mu}{\langle}T_{\nu)\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}\\
&+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)-a_{0}{\epsilon}_{\sigma\rho(\mu}p_{4\nu)}p_{4}^{\sigma}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(p_{1})J_{\beta}(p_{2})J_{\gamma}(p_{3})J^{\rho}(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}=0\end{aligned}

(5.41)

To test this relation with correlators at our disposal, we choose
\mu=3
,
\alpha=\beta=+
,
\gamma=\nu=-
and work in the collinear limit. The equation then simplifies to:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &-\frac{ia_{1}}{2}\bigg(p_{1}^{2}{\langle}O(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}+p_{2}^{2}{\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})O(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}\bigg)\\
&+\frac{ia_{2}}{2}p_{1}{\langle}T_{+-}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}+\frac{ia_{2}}{2}p_{2}{\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})T_{+-}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}\\
&+\frac{ia_{2}}{2}p_{3}{\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})T_{--}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}-\frac{ia_{0}}{2}p_{4}^{2}{\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})J_{-}(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}=0\end{aligned}

(5.42)

Similarly to
{\langle}[Q_{3},J_{1}OOO]{\rangle}_{FF}
, we can analyze the (
5.42
) for different values of
p_{1,2,3}
. We confine ourselves to analyzing the
p_{1}>0
,
p_{2}>0
,
p_{3}>0
. It’s straightforward to check that the conclusions carry over to the more general case. The terms of the Ward identity become:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} p_{1}^{2}{\langle}O(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}=&\frac{p_{1}^{2}}{4p_{4}}+\frac{p_{1}^{2}p_{2}p_{3}}{4(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\
p_{3}{\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})T_{--}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}=&-\frac{ip_{1}p_{2}p_{3}^{2}}{16(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\
p_{4}^{2}{\langle}J_{+}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})J_{-}(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}=&\frac{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}{8(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\
p_{1}{\langle}T_{+-}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{-}(p_{3})O(p_{4}){\rangle}_{FF}=&0\end{aligned}

(5.43)

The non-contact terms of the Ward identity (
5.42
) then amount to:

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}(2ia_{0}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})+4ia_{1}(p_{1}+p_{2})-a_{2}p_{3})}{32(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}=0

(5.44)

Which is uniquely satisfied for
a_{2}=2ia_{0}
and
a_{0}=-2a_{1}
. These constraints are consistent with those of Sections
2
. The remaining terms are all polynomials in three of the four momenta:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\langle}[Q_{3-},J_{+}J_{+}J_{-}O]{\rangle}_{FF}|_{\text{contact terms}}=-\frac{ia_{1}}{8}\bigg(\frac{p_{1}^{2}}{p_{4}}+\frac{p_{2}^{2}}{p_{4}}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(5.45)

We argue that these terms originate from contact terms of the full covariant expression, and ought to be absorbed into some of the correlators, for the Ward identity to be fully consistent. After imposing the FF algebra constraints, one can verify that (
5.42
) is consistent in all other kinematic regimes, once appropriate contact terms are subtracted from the correlators. Writing these contact terms in closed form just from the collinear limit expressions is tedious, however it is obvious that higher-spin symmetry imposes constraints on contact terms of different four-point functions.

6
Summary and Discussion

We summarize the main results of previous sections and detail the possibilities for follow-ups of this work:

6.1
Local higher-spin algebra

The higher-spin algebra of QF Chern-Simons-matter theory is constrained by analyzing local Ward idenitities of three-point functions, for operators of spin
s<4
. By construction, it reproduces the higher-spin Ward identities of singlet sectors of the free-fermionic
U(N)
model and of the critical-bosonic
U(N)
model, in the limits
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0
and
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to\infty
, respectively. The former is verified by making free field contractions (see Appendix
C.4
), while the latter is verified by a resummation of Feynman diagrams at large
N
(see Section
3
). Under the insertion of the spin-3 current
(\partial\cdot J)^{QF}_{\mu\nu}(x)
, the local variations of the scalar and vector current are given by:

\displaystyle\delta O^{QF}(x_{1})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\delta O^{FF}(x_{1})

(6.1)

\displaystyle\delta J^{QF}_{\alpha}(x_{2})=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\delta_{O}J^{FF}_{\alpha}(x_{2})+\frac{\delta_{T}J^{FF}(x_{2})+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}\delta_{T}J^{CB}(x_{2})}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}+\frac{h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{{\tilde{N}}}\delta(x_{2}-x){\epsilon}_{\alpha\rho(\mu}J_{\nu)}J^{\rho}(x)

(6.2)

Where
\delta O^{FF},\delta_{O}J^{FF},\delta_{T}J^{FF}
and
\delta_{T}J^{CB}=\delta_{T}J^{FB}
stand in for linear combinations of contact terms, defined in (
4.8
) and (
3.10
), respectively. The divergence of the spin-3 current is given by (
3.28
) in our normalization, and is consistent with
[
14
]
.
h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})
is an even function of the ’t Hooft coupling, which vanishes in the free/critical limits and is unfixed by our analysis. The action of the spin-3 pseudocharge
Q_{\mu\nu}
on spin-0 and spin-1 currents is now given by:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &[Q_{\mu\nu},O]_{QF}=\int_{x}\delta O^{QF}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{8}{3}{\epsilon}_{\alpha\beta(\mu}\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\alpha}J^{\beta}\\[5.0pt]
&[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}]_{QF}=\int_{x}\delta J^{QF}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}\frac{4}{3}{\epsilon}_{\sigma\alpha(\mu}\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\sigma}O+\frac{16i}{3}\frac{1-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\partial_{(\mu}T_{\nu)\alpha}+\frac{h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{{\tilde{N}}}{\epsilon}_{\alpha\rho(\mu}J_{\nu)}J^{\rho}\end{aligned}

(6.3)

Our result (
6.3
) is similar to the form of commutators guessed in
[
38
]
and builds on it by fixing the exact coefficients of each term. Furthermore, as we’ve shown, the analysis at this level still allows for the existence of a double-trace term in
[Q_{3},J]_{QF}
, which was not originally present in
[
38
]
.

Pertaining to the QF theory discussed in this text, the algebra (
4.19
) can be used to investigate
n
-point functions of currents with spin
s\leq 3
, for
n>4
. Combining this approach with bootstrap techniques
[
48
]
may lead to stronger constraints on higher correlators of the theory. Further algebraic constraints can also be obtained. One may discuss Ward identities which contain the action of the spin-3 pseudocharge on operators of spin
s>1
. Moreover, it would also be interesting to consider Ward identities of
Q_{s>3}
pseudocharges. This was already done for integrated spin-4 identities in several instances
[
13
,
38
,
39
]
. Indeed, the compelling premise of higher-spin symmetry is that an arbitrary number of constraints on a specific correlator can be obtained. A further understanding of the interplay of different higher-spin Ward identities is in principle limited only by computational difficulty. In the case of free theories, extending the approach of Section
2
for higher-spin currents should be straightforward. The Ward identities may also be used to constrain correlation functions of non-local operators
[
18
,
19
,
20
]
.

Another natural direction is to investigate the constraints of higher-spin symmetry in quasibosonic Chern-Simons-matter theories
[
23
,
42
]
, as well as generalizations with bosons and fermions gauged by Chern-Simons interactions
[
49
,
5
]
. Such theories are closely related to the QF model discussed here, hence establishing their higher-spin algebras could lead to further checks on correlators, both at separated and coincident points. Furthermore, it would be interesting to see if such current algebra methods can be extended to theories with
\mathcal{N}=2
supersymmetry
[
7
,
6
]
. It would also be insightful to study higher-spin Ward identities along the full flow between free-bosonic and critical-bosonic vector models, at large
N
. This would require extending the analysis of Sections
2
and
3
to theories where conformal symmetry is broken.

Finally, it would be interesting to examine whether higher-spin symmetry imposes constraints on subleading terms in the large
N
expansion. This would involve the computation of non-planar corrections to correlation functions, as well as the analysis of Ward identities at order
\mathcal{O}(1/N)
.

6.2
Results for quasifermionic four-point functions

Four-point functions involving a single insertion of
(\partial\cdot J_{3})^{QF}
are decomposed into two- and three-point correlators of free/critical theories using higher-spin Ward identities, as described in Section
4.2
. We write the form of these correlators schematically, excluding Chern-Simons contact terms:

\displaystyle{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}OO{\rangle}_{QF}=\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}\bigg({\langle}...{\rangle}_{FF}+{\tilde{\lambda}}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{odd}+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{CB}\bigg)

(6.4)

\displaystyle{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}=\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}\bigg({\langle}...{\rangle}_{FF}+{\tilde{\lambda}}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{X_{1}}+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{X_{2}}+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{3}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{X_{3}}+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{4}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{CB}+h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\langle}...{\rangle}_{X_{4}}\bigg)

(6.5)

Four-point functions with specific Lorentz indices were also computed directly in the FF+CS description, in the limit of collinear momenta (see Sections
5.2
and
5.3
). The non-contact terms of these correlators are:

\displaystyle{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{QF}=\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}^{3}}\bigg({\langle}...{\rangle}_{FF}+{\tilde{\lambda}}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{X_{1}}+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{X_{2}}+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{3}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{CB}\bigg)

(6.6)

\displaystyle{\langle}J_{---}J_{+}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{QF}=\frac{{\tilde{N}}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}\bigg({\langle}...{\rangle}_{FF}+{\tilde{\lambda}}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{X_{1}}+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{X_{2}}+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{3}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{X_{3}}+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{4}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{CB}\bigg)\par

(6.7)

With
{\langle}J_{---}J_{+}J_{+}J_{+}{\rangle}_{QF}
containing an additional contact term, as observed in (
5.22
). We highlight some features of these results:

•

The
{\tilde{\lambda}}
-dependence of
{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{QF}
is inconsistent with the form conjectured in
[
38
]
, while the
{\tilde{\lambda}}
-dependence of
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
is consistent with the form conjectured therein.

•

We do not observe any relations between the four terms of (
6.6
). This suggests that the full correlator
{\langle}T_{\mu\nu}J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}O{\rangle}_{QF}
contains four independent non-local structures, outside the collinear limit. The Ward identity analysis (
4.20
) suggests that the four-point function
{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
may satisfy an epsilon-transform relation with respect to the vector current (
4.29
), consistent with the conjecture of
[
38
]
. It would be interesting to check these statements directly, which requires either a more complicated perturbative calculation, or an alternate approach.

•

The form of
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
is consistent between the spin-3 Ward identity (
6.5
) and direct perturbative result (
6.7
). The Ward identity contains an additional disconnected piece, multiplied by an even function
26
26

26

h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})
vanishes as
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0,\infty
, so it does not affect the free/critical theory results.

h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})
, which was not fixed by our analysis. It would be interesting to check if this function vanishes, as anticipated in
[
38
]
. In the collinear limit, the five structures of
{\langle}J_{3}J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
satisfy an epsilon-transform relation (
5.24
), and the Ward identity analysis (
4.27
) suggests that this property may hold for general momenta. We note that the same (and, in fact, slightly stronger) property was observed in
[
41
]
, for the five structures of
{\langle}J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
, in the collinear limit. This could imply that such a property holds for general correlators of the form
{\langle}J_{s}J_{1}J_{1}J_{1}{\rangle}_{QF}
, and perhaps for more general four-point functions of spinning currents.

Although the momentum space relations for the structures of four-point functions (
6.4
) and (
6.5
) appear intricate, we emphasize that these relations simplify when written in spinor-helicity variables, as is usually the case for correlators of massless fields. A better understanding of the structure of QF four-point functions would allow for direct comparisons to dual higher-spin gravity theories
[
10
,
50
]
and further investigation of the chiral limits discussed in
[
36
,
37
]
.

6.3
Background Chern-Simons contact terms

Imposing higher-spin symmetry at coincident points implies that higher correlators develop scheme-independent contact terms related to the presence of the background Chern-Simons term in the effective action of the theory, as discussed in Section
4.3
. Interestingly, these contact terms have different dependences on the ’t Hooft coupling, depending on whether we are working in the FF+CS description (
4.1
), or the CB+CS description (
4.2
). By analyzing higher-spin Ward identities, we argue that the following correlators develop scheme-independent contact terms. Schematically:

\displaystyle{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}J_{\alpha}O{\rangle}_{QF}|_{CS\text{ contact terms}}=\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{ct_{1}}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\tilde{\lambda}}}{\sqrt{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{ct_{2}}

(6.8)

\displaystyle{\langle}(\partial\cdot J)_{\mu\nu}J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}J_{\gamma}{\rangle}_{QF}|_{CS\text{ contact terms}}=\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\tilde{\lambda}}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{ct_{1}}+\frac{{\tilde{N}}g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2}}{\langle}...{\rangle}_{ct_{2}}+{\tilde{N}}h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}}){\langle}...{\rangle}_{ct_{3}}

(6.9)

Where
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})={\tilde{\lambda}}
in the FF+CS description and
g_{1}({\tilde{\lambda}})=-{\tilde{\lambda}}^{-1}
in the CB+CS description and
h_{3}({\tilde{\lambda}})
is again, an unfixed even function. Although some of these terms appear divergent in the
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0,\infty
limits, they are not present in FF and CB theories. We also note that the correlator
{\langle}(\partial\cdot J_{3})J_{1}OO{\rangle}_{QF}
contains a higher-spin contact term in the CB+CS description (
4.36
), but not in the FF+CS description. Similar statements can be made for correlators of the quasibosonic Chern-Simons-matter theory (see Section
4.3
).

The perturbative results of Section
5
imply the presence of additional parity-breaking contact terms. We infer the Lorentz covariant form of these terms from collinear limit results
27
27

27

The underlines and overlines denote index symmetrizations.
:

\displaystyle{\langle}T_{\mu\nu}(-q_{1})J_{\alpha}(-q_{2})J_{\beta}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}|_{\text{contact terms}}={\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}\frac{1}{64}g_{\alpha(\mu}{\epsilon}_{\nu)\beta\rho}q_{1}^{\rho}+(\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta)

(6.10)

\displaystyle{\langle}J_{\mu\nu\rho}(-q_{4})J_{\alpha}(-q_{1})J_{\beta}(-q_{2})J_{\gamma}(-q_{3}){\rangle}_{QF}|_{\text{contact terms}}=\frac{{\tilde{N}}{\tilde{\lambda}}^{5}}{(1+{\tilde{\lambda}}^{2})^{2}}g_{\underline{\alpha}\overline{\mu}}g_{\underline{\beta}\overline{\nu}}{\epsilon}_{\sigma\underline{\gamma}\overline{\rho}}\frac{q_{4}^{\sigma}}{6}

(6.11)

We emphasize that this computation is done in the FF+CS description. These terms are similar to the
U(1)
Chern-Simons term appearing in
{\langle}J_{\mu}J_{\nu}{\rangle}_{QF}
and it would be useful to better understand how they complement contact terms found in (
6.8
) and (
6.9
) and if they can be interpreted as arising from other Ward identities. In particular, it is not obvious if the contact terms present in (
6.11
) are the same terms appearing in (
6.9
).

Chern-Simons contact terms of various QF correlators correspond to generalized
\theta
-terms in the action of the bulk dual higher-spin gravity. In this work, we have only considered contact terms of the vector current two-point function, which corresponds to a specific tuning of the bulk
F\wedge F
term, however the analysis is by no means complete
28
28

28

Similarly, one can analyze relations imposed by contact terms in
{\langle}TT{\rangle}_{QF}
, associated to a gravitational Chern-Simons term
R\wedge R
in the bulk action
[
17
]
, as well as more complicated cases.
. A better understanding of the map between contact terms (
6.8
)-(
6.11
) and bulk
\theta
-terms would serve to further probe higher-spin gravity duals of Chern-Simons vector models. It would be interesting to analyze the effects of these terms on higher-point correlators in gravity-side computations, as well as to understand to what extent they affect higher-point correlators in the chiral limits discussed in
[
36
,
37
]
. In the main text, it was argued that the three-point function contact terms (
6.8
) and (
6.10
) do not contribute in these limits, which is consistent with the expected matching between CFT three-point functions and cubic vertices in the bulk.

Appendix A
{\langle}T_{--}J_{+}J_{+}O{\rangle}_{QF}
: The full expression

We find four distinct structures of this correlator, as defined in (
5.19
). We write the result in terms of collinear momenta
p_{i}=-q_{i}
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}T_{--}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{+}(p_{3})O(-p_{1}-p_{2}-p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}=\\
&-\frac{ip_{1}^{3}{\text{sign}}(p_{1})(p_{1}(p_{2}+p_{3})+p_{2}^{2}+p_{2}p_{3}+p_{3}^{2})}{32p_{2}p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}+\frac{i(p_{1}-p_{3})(p_{1}+p_{3})^{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{3})}{32p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\\
&+\frac{ip_{2}^{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{2})(p_{1}^{2}(p_{2}+2p_{3})+p_{1}(p_{2}^{2}+3p_{2}p_{3}+2p_{3}^{2})+p_{2}p_{3}(p_{2}+p_{3}))}{32p_{1}p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}-\frac{i(p_{2}+p_{3})^{3}{\text{sign}}(p_{2}+p_{3})}{32p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\\
&+\frac{ip_{3}^{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{3})(p_{1}^{2}(2p_{2}+p_{3})+p_{1}(2p_{2}^{2}+3p_{2}p_{3}+p_{3}^{2})+p_{2}p_{3}(p_{2}+p_{3}))}{32p_{1}p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\
&+\frac{i(p_{1}-p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{2})^{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{2})}{32p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}-\frac{i(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}(p_{1}^{3}(p_{2}+p_{3})+p_{1}^{2}p_{2}p_{3}){\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}{32p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\
&-\frac{i(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}(-p_{1}(p_{2}^{3}+p_{2}^{2}p_{3}+p_{2}p_{3}^{2}+p_{3}^{3})-p_{2}p_{3}(p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}){\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}{32p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\
\\
\cline{1-2}\cr\\
&{\langle}T_{--}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{+}(p_{3})O(-p_{1}-p_{2}-p_{3}){\rangle}_{X_{1}}=\\
&\frac{p_{3}^{3}\left(2p_{1}^{2}+p_{1}(p_{2}+p_{3})+p_{2}(p_{3}-2p_{2})\right)}{32p_{1}p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}-\frac{4p_{1}^{2}p_{3}^{2}+(p_{1}-p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{2})^{3}}{32p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\
&+\frac{-p_{1}^{4}-4p_{1}^{3}p_{2}+p_{2}^{4}}{32p_{1}p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\par-\frac{p_{3}\left(p_{1}^{4}+3p_{1}^{2}p_{2}^{2}+3p_{1}p_{2}^{2}(p_{2}+p_{3})+p_{2}^{3}(p_{2}+3p_{3})\right)}{16p_{1}p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\

&+\frac{p_{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{2})\left(2p_{1}^{3}(p_{2}+p_{3})+p_{1}^{2}\left(3p_{2}^{2}+4p_{2}p_{3}+2p_{3}^{2}\right)\right){\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}{32p_{1}p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\
&+\frac{p_{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{2})\left(p_{1}p_{2}\left(p_{2}^{2}+3p_{2}p_{3}+2p_{3}^{2}\right)+p_{2}^{2}p_{3}(p_{2}+p_{3})\right){\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}{32p_{1}p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\
&+\frac{p_{3}{\text{sign}}(p_{3})\left(p_{1}p_{3}\left(2p_{2}^{2}+3p_{2}p_{3}+p_{3}^{2}\right)+p_{2}p_{3}^{2}(p_{2}+p_{3})\right){\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}{32p_{1}p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{3})(p_{2}+p_{3})}\\

&-\frac{p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{3}){\text{sign}}(p_{3}){\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{3})}{16p_{1}p_{2}}+\frac{(p_{1}-p_{2})(p_{1}+p_{2})^{2}{\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{2}){\text{sign}}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}{32p_{1}p_{2}p_{3}}\\

\cline{1-2}\cr\\
\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}T_{--}(p_{1})J_{+}(p_{2})J_{+}(p_{3})O(-p_{1}-p_{2}-p_{3}){\rangle}_{X_{2}}=\\

Appendix C
Supplementary computations

We present the more technical computations referenced throughout the main text.

C.1
Integrated FF Ward identities

Continuing subsection
2.4.1
, we verify that the higher-spin algebra (
2.15
), (
2.17
) obtained through the free-fermionic OPE leads to consistent higher-spin Ward identities. We consider the action of the spin-3 charge
Q_{\mu\nu}
on a general
n
-point function of
m-1
vector currents and
n-m+1
scalar currents, denoted by
{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha_{1}}J_{\alpha_{2}}...J_{\alpha_{m-1}}O...O]{\rangle}_{FF}
.

Specially, the result also holds when the number of scalar or vector currents is zero. Furthermore, when
m
is odd, the Ward identity is trivially satisfied as all correlators vanish due to charge conjugation.

For simplicity, we will assume the algebra only contains terms appearing in (
2.15
), (
2.17
) with unknown coefficients, and then find constraints for the Ward identity to be satisfied. The reader is welcome to start from the more general ansatz (
2.6
), and verify that it leads to the same result. The Ward identity (
2.33
) reads:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\bigg(a_{1}\epsilon_{\sigma\alpha_{1}(\mu}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}{\langle}O(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})O(x_{m})...O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}\\[5.0pt]
&+a_{2}\partial_{1(\mu}{\langle}T_{\nu)\alpha_{1}}(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})O(x_{m})...O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha_{1}\leftrightarrow\alpha_{2}\end{Bmatrix}+..+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow m-1\\
\alpha_{1}\leftrightarrow\alpha_{m-1}\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\\[5.0pt]
&+a_{0}\bigg(\epsilon_{\rho\sigma(\mu}\partial_{m\nu)}\partial_{m}^{\rho}{\langle}J_{\alpha_{1}}(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})J^{\sigma}(x_{m})O(x_{m+1})...O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}+\begin{Bmatrix}m\leftrightarrow m+1\\
\alpha_{m}\leftrightarrow\alpha_{m+1}\end{Bmatrix}+\\[5.0pt]
&...+\begin{Bmatrix}m\leftrightarrow n\\
\alpha_{m}\leftrightarrow\alpha_{n}\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)=0\end{aligned}

(C.1)

We will define
\sum_{(i)}
to be the sum over all permutations
\{p_{1},p_{2},..,p_{n}\}
of
\{1,2,...,n\}
where
p_{i}=1
and
\{p_{2},..,p_{n}\}
is a permutation of
\{1,2,...,n\}/\{i\}
. We also define
{\Gamma}_{p_{i}}={\gamma}_{\alpha_{p_{i}}}
if
p_{i}<m
and
{\Gamma}_{p_{i}}=1
if
p_{i}\geq m
. Finally, the fermionic propagator from
x_{i}
to
x_{j}
is denoted by
S_{ij}
. The first term in (
C.1
) can now be written as:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\epsilon_{\sigma\alpha_{1}(\mu}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}{\langle}O(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})O(x_{m})..O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}\\[5.0pt]
&=\par-i^{m-2}\sum_{(1)}\epsilon_{\sigma\alpha_{p_{1}}(\mu}\partial_{p_{1}\nu)}\partial_{p_{1}}^{\sigma}Tr\big(S_{p_{n}p_{1}}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}S_{p_{2}p_{3}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\big)\\
&=\par-i^{m-1}\sum_{(1)}\epsilon_{\sigma\alpha_{p_{1}}(\mu}\partial_{p_{1}\nu)}\partial_{p_{1}\rho}Tr\bigg(S_{p_{n}p_{1}}\frac{1}{2}\{{\gamma}^{\rho},{\gamma}^{\sigma}\}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}S_{p_{2}p_{3}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(C.2)

It is simple to show that this can be rewritten as

\epsilon_{\sigma\alpha_{1}(\mu}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}{\langle}O(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})O(x_{m})..O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}=A_{1}-B_{1}
, where:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} A_{k}&=i^{m-1}\sum_{(k)}Tr\big(\partial_{p_{1}\mu}\partial_{p_{1}\nu}S_{p_{n}p_{1}}{\Gamma}_{p_{1}}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}-S_{p_{n}p_{1}}{\Gamma}_{p_{1}}\partial_{p_{1}\mu}\partial_{p_{1}\nu}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\big)\\
B_{k}&=-i^{m-1}\sum_{(k)}\partial_{p_{1}(\nu}Tr\big(\partial_{p_{1}\alpha_{p_{1}}}S_{p_{n}p_{1}}{\gamma}_{\mu)}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}-S_{p_{n}p_{1}}{\gamma}_{\mu)}\partial_{p_{1}\alpha_{p_{1}}}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\big)\par\end{aligned}

(C.3)

The stress tensor terms can also be rewritten, by expanding the operators (
1.4
) and making contractions:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\partial_{1(\mu}{\langle}T_{\nu)\alpha_{1}}(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})O(x_{m})...O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}\\[5.0pt]
&=\par\frac{i^{m-2}}{4}\sum_{(1)}\partial_{p_{1}(\mu}Tr\big(S_{p_{n}p_{1}}{\gamma}_{\nu)}\partial_{p_{1}\alpha_{p_{1}}}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}-\partial_{p_{1}\alpha_{p_{1}}}S_{p_{n}p_{1}}{\gamma}_{\nu}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\big)\\
&\quad\par+\frac{i^{m-2}}{4}\sum_{(1)}\partial_{p_{1}(\mu}Tr\big(S_{p_{n}p_{1}}{\Gamma}_{p_{1}}\partial_{p_{1}\nu)}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{1}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}-\partial_{p_{1}\nu)}S_{p_{n}p_{1}}{\Gamma}_{p_{1}}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\par\big)=\frac{i}{4}A_{1}+\frac{i}{4}B_{1}\par\end{aligned}

(C.4)

The remaining terms in (
C.1
) are those coming from
[Q_{\mu\nu},O]_{FF}
.

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\epsilon_{\rho\sigma(\mu}\partial_{m\nu)}\partial_{m}^{\rho}{\langle}J_{\alpha_{1}}(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})J^{\sigma}(x_{m})O(x_{m+1})...O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}\\[5.0pt]
&=\par-i^{m}\sum_{(m)}\epsilon_{\rho\sigma(\mu}\partial_{p_{1}\nu)}\partial_{p_{1}}^{\rho}Tr\big(S_{p_{n}p_{1}}{\gamma}^{\sigma}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\big)\\
&=\par-i^{m-1}\sum_{(m)}\partial_{p_{1}(\nu}\partial_{p_{1}}^{\rho}Tr\bigg(S_{p_{n}p_{1}}\frac{1}{2}[{\gamma}_{\mu)},{\gamma}_{\rho}]S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\bigg)\\
&=\par-i^{m-1}\sum_{(m)}\partial_{p_{1}(\nu}Tr\bigg(\partial_{p_{1}}^{\rho}S_{p_{n}p_{1}}\frac{1}{2}[{\gamma}_{\mu)},{\gamma}_{\rho}]S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}+S_{p_{n}p_{1}}\frac{1}{2}[{\gamma}_{\mu)},{\gamma}_{\rho}]\partial_{p_{1}}^{\rho}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\bigg)\end{aligned}

(C.5)

We can now replace commutators by anticommutators. This modifies (
C.5
) by adding terms proportional to the equations of motion, and thus does not change non-local terms
29
29

29

Here we are only interested in matching non-contact terms. Contact terms can also be included tracking them throughout the derivation.
. We find:

\displaystyle\epsilon_{\rho\sigma(\mu}\partial_{m\nu)}\partial_{m}^{\rho}{\langle}J_{\alpha_{1}}(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})J^{\sigma}(x_{m})O(x_{m+1})...O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}

\displaystyle=\par-i^{m-1}\sum_{(m)}\partial_{p_{1}(\nu}Tr\big(\partial_{p_{1}\mu)}S_{p_{n}p_{1}}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}-S_{p_{n}p_{1}}\partial_{p_{1}\mu)}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\big)

\displaystyle=\par-i^{m-1}\sum_{(m)}Tr\big(\partial_{p_{1}\mu}\partial_{p_{1}\nu}S_{p_{n}p_{1}}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}-S_{p_{n}p_{1}}\partial_{p_{1}\mu}\partial_{p_{1}\nu}S_{p_{1}p_{2}}{\Gamma}_{p_{2}}...{\Gamma}_{p_{n}}\big)=-A_{m}\par

Finally, one can verify that
A_{k}
’s satisfy the following relation:

\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}A_{k}+\sum_{k=m}^{n}A_{k}=0\implies\sum_{k=m}^{n}A_{k}=-\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}A_{k}

(C.6)

We insert these expressions into the Ward identity (
C.1
), to obtain:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\bigg(a_{1}\epsilon_{\sigma\alpha_{1}(\mu}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}{\langle}O(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})O(x_{m})...O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}\\[5.0pt]
&+a_{2}\partial_{1(\mu}{\langle}T_{\nu)\alpha_{1}}(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})O(x_{m})...O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha_{1}\leftrightarrow\alpha_{2}\end{Bmatrix}+...+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow m-1\\
\alpha_{1}\leftrightarrow\alpha_{m-1}\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\\[5.0pt]
&+a_{0}\bigg(\epsilon_{\rho\sigma(\mu}\partial_{m\nu)}\partial_{m}^{\rho}{\langle}J_{\alpha_{1}}(x_{1})J_{\alpha_{2}}(x_{2})..J_{\alpha_{m-1}}(x_{m-1})J^{\sigma}(x_{m})O(x_{m+1})...O(x_{n}){\rangle}_{FF}+\begin{Bmatrix}m\leftrightarrow m+1\\
\alpha_{m}\leftrightarrow\alpha_{m+1}\end{Bmatrix}+\\[5.0pt]
&...+\begin{Bmatrix}m\leftrightarrow n\\
\alpha_{m}\leftrightarrow\alpha_{n}\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)=\par\bigg(a_{1}+\frac{ia_{2}}{4}+a_{0}\bigg)\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}A_{k}+\bigg(-a_{1}+\frac{ia_{2}}{4}\bigg)\sum_{k=1}^{m-1}B_{k}=0\par\end{aligned}

(C.7)

Requiring that the equation is consistent for all
n
and
m
and for generic
x_{i}
gives us a system of two linear homogenous equations. We solve this to find
a_{1}=-\frac{a_{0}}{2}=\frac{a}{2}
and
a_{2}=-4ia_{1}=-2ia
which is consistent with the coefficients obtained in Section
2.2
.

C.2
Integrated FB Ward identities

In this subsection we verify that the algebra coefficients in (
2.24
) and (
2.26
) lead to consistent free-bosonic higher-spin Ward identities. We confine ourselves to Ward identities of three-point functions, however extending the analysis to general
n
-point functions should be straightforward.

There are four different Ward identities one can consider. However, two of these, specifically
{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},OOO]{\rangle}_{FB}
and
{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}O]{\rangle}_{FB}
are trivially satisfied, as all appearing correlators are zero, due to charge conjugation. Assuming general coefficients in (
2.24
) and (
2.26
), we obtain constraints on the algebra from the two non-trivial Ward identities:

\underline{\bm{{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}OO]{\rangle}_{FB}}\textbf{ Ward identity}}

The equation reads:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &b_{1}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}{\langle}O(x_{1})O(x_{2})O(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}+b_{2}g_{\alpha(\mu}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1}^{2}{\langle}O(x_{1})O(x_{2})O(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}\\[5.0pt]
&+b_{3}\partial_{1(\mu}{\langle}T_{\nu)\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2})O(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}+b_{0}\big(\partial_{2(\mu}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})J_{\nu)}(x_{2})O(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}\\[5.0pt]
&+\partial_{3(\mu}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2})J_{\nu)}(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}\big)=0\end{aligned}

(C.8)

We denote the free scalar propagator from
x_{i}
to
x_{j}
by
F_{ij}
. Terms involving the scalar three-point function
{\langle}OOO{\rangle}_{FB}=8^{3}\cdot 2F_{12}F_{23}F_{31}
can be written as:

\displaystyle\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}{\langle}O(x_{1})O(x_{2})O(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}=\par

\displaystyle 8^{3}\cdot 2\big(\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{12}F_{23}F_{31}+F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{31}\big)

\displaystyle+8^{3}\cdot 6\big(\partial_{1(\alpha}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu)}F_{31}+\partial_{1(\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\alpha)}F_{31}\big)

(C.9)

\displaystyle g_{\alpha(\mu}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1}^{2}{\langle}O(x_{1})O(x_{2})O(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}=

\displaystyle 8^{3}\cdot 4g_{\alpha(\mu}(\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1\sigma}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}F_{31}+\partial_{1\sigma}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}F_{31})

(C.10)

Using the definition of the stress-tensor (
1.2
), we similarly write out the term involving
{\langle}TOO{\rangle}_{FB}
, while neglecting contact terms that come from the equations of motion:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\partial_{1(\mu}{\langle}T_{\nu)\alpha}(x_{1})O(x_{2})O(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}=\\
&8^{2}\bigg(-\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{1\alpha}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{12}F_{23}F_{31}+F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{31})\par+\frac{3}{2}(\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{31}+\partial_{1\alpha}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{31})\\
&+(\partial_{1\alpha}\partial_{1(\nu}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\mu)}F_{31}+\partial_{1(\mu}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{31})\par-g_{\alpha(\mu}(\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1\sigma}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}F_{31}+\partial_{1\sigma}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}F_{31})\bigg)\end{aligned}

(C.11)

And analogously, we write out the terms coming from
[Q_{\mu\nu},O]_{FB}
as:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\partial_{2(\mu}{\langle}J_{\alpha}(x_{1})J_{\nu)}(x_{2})O(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}+\begin{Bmatrix}2\leftrightarrow 3\end{Bmatrix}=\\
&8\bigg(2(\partial_{1\alpha}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{12}F_{23}F_{31}+F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{31})-2(\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{31}+\partial_{1\alpha}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{31})\bigg)\end{aligned}

(C.12)

Inserting the relations (
C.9
) - (
C.12
) into the Ward identity (
C.8
), we obtain a sum of linearly independent terms:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &(2b_{0}+128b_{1}-4b_{3})\bigg(\partial_{1\alpha}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{12}F_{23}F_{31}+F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{31}\bigg)+\\
&(-2b_{0}+128b_{1}+12b_{3})\bigg(\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{31}+\partial_{1\alpha}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{31}\bigg)\\
&+(256b_{1}+8b_{3})\bigg(\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{31}+\partial_{1\alpha}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{31}\bigg)\\
&+(256b_{2}-8b_{3})g_{\alpha(\mu}\bigg(\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1\sigma}F_{12}F_{23}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}F_{31}+\partial_{1\sigma}F_{23}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}F_{31}\bigg)=0\end{aligned}

(C.13)

Since the terms are linearly independent, the consistency of this equation implies
b_{1}=-\frac{b_{0}}{128}
,
b_{2}=-b_{1}=\frac{b_{0}}{128}
,
b_{3}=-32b_{1}=\frac{b_{0}}{4}
, which are the same constraints obtained through the free-bosonic OPE (
2.26
).

\underline{\bm{{\langle}[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}J_{\beta}J_{\gamma}]{\rangle}_{FB}}\textbf{ Ward identity}}

Inserting (
2.26
) into (
2.38
), we have the integrated Ward identity:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &b_{1}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}{\langle}O(x_{1})J_{\beta}(x_{2})J_{\gamma}(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}+b_{2}g_{\alpha(\mu}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1}^{2}{\langle}O(x_{1})J_{\beta}(x_{2})J_{\gamma}(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}\\
&+b_{3}\partial_{1(\mu}{\langle}T_{\nu)\alpha}(x_{1})J_{\beta}(x_{2})J_{\gamma}(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}=0\end{aligned}

(C.14)

The quickest way to verify this is to expand (
2.26
) using the definitions of current operators (
1.2
):

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &[Q_{\mu\nu},J_{\alpha}]=\\[5.0pt]
&\bigg(8b_{1}-\frac{1}{4}b_{3}\bigg)(\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}{\phi^{\dagger}}{\phi}+{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}\partial_{\alpha}{\phi})+\bigg(8b_{1}+\frac{3}{4}b_{3}\bigg)(\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{\alpha}{\phi}+\partial_{\alpha}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{\mu}\partial_{\nu}{\phi})\\
&+\bigg(8b_{1}+\frac{1}{4}b_{3}\bigg)(\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{(\mu}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{\nu)}{\phi}+\partial_{(\nu}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{\mu)}\partial_{\alpha}{\phi})+\bigg(16b_{2}-\frac{1}{2}b_{3}\bigg)g_{\alpha(\mu}(\partial_{\sigma}\partial_{\nu)}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial^{\sigma}{\phi}+\partial_{\sigma}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{\nu)}\partial^{\sigma}{\phi})\end{aligned}

(C.15)

Let us now look at contractions that come from each term separately, and then sum over permutations. Doing so, we find that the first two terms in (
C.15
) lead to the same contributions, which we will label
A_{1}
:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &A_{1}={\langle}(\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}{\phi^{\dagger}}{\phi}+{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}{\phi})(x_{1})J_{\beta}(x_{2})J_{\gamma}(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}=\\
&\quad={\langle}(\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{1\alpha}{\phi}+\partial_{1\alpha}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}{\phi})(x_{1})J_{\beta}(x_{2})J_{\gamma}(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 2\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\beta\end{Bmatrix}+\begin{Bmatrix}1\leftrightarrow 3\\
\alpha\leftrightarrow\gamma\end{Bmatrix}=\\
&=-2\bigg(\partial_{1\alpha}\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}F_{12}(\partial_{2\beta}F_{23}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{31}-F_{31}\partial_{2\beta}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{23})+\partial_{1\mu}\partial_{1\nu}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{13}(\partial_{3\gamma}F_{32}\partial_{2\beta}F_{21}-F_{21}\partial_{2\beta}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{32})\\
&\qquad+\partial_{2\mu}\partial_{2\nu}\partial_{2\beta}F_{21}(\partial_{1\alpha}F_{13}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{32}-F_{32}\partial_{1\alpha}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{13})+\partial_{2\mu}\partial_{2\nu}\partial_{2\beta}F_{23}(\partial_{3\gamma}F_{31}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{12}-F_{12}\partial_{3\gamma}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{31})\\
&\qquad+\partial_{3\mu}\partial_{3\nu}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{32}(\partial_{2\beta}F_{21}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{13}-F_{13}\partial_{2\beta}\partial_{1\alpha}F_{21})+\partial_{3\mu}\partial_{3\nu}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{31}(\partial_{1\alpha}F_{12}\partial_{2\beta}F_{23}-F_{23}\partial_{1\alpha}\partial_{2\beta}F_{12})\bigg)\end{aligned}

(C.16)

The remaining terms in (
C.15
) give different contributions, which we label
A_{2}
and
A_{3}
:

\displaystyle A_{2}={\langle}(\partial_{1\alpha}\partial_{1(\mu}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{1\nu)}{\phi}+\partial_{1(\mu}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{1\nu)}\partial_{1\alpha}{\phi})(x_{1})J_{\beta}(x_{2})J_{\gamma}(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}=

\displaystyle\qquad-2\partial_{1\alpha}\big(\partial_{1(\mu}F_{12}\partial_{2\beta}F_{23}\partial_{3\gamma}\partial_{1\nu)}F_{31}+\partial_{1(\mu}F_{13}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{32}\partial_{2\beta}\partial_{1\nu)}F_{21}

(C.17)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-\partial_{1(\mu}F_{12}\partial_{2\beta}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{23}\partial_{1\nu)}F_{31}-\partial_{1(\mu}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{13}F_{32}\partial_{2\beta}\partial_{1\nu)}F_{21}\big)

\displaystyle A_{3}={\langle}(\partial_{1\sigma}\partial_{1\alpha}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}{\phi}+\partial_{1\sigma}{\phi^{\dagger}}\partial_{1\alpha}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}{\phi})(x_{1})J_{\beta}(x_{2})J_{\gamma}(x_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}=

\displaystyle\qquad-2\partial_{1\alpha}(\partial_{1\sigma}F_{12}\partial_{2\beta}F_{23}\partial_{3\gamma}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}F_{31}+\partial_{1\sigma}F_{13}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{32}\partial_{2\beta}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}F_{21}

(C.18)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\quad-\partial_{1\sigma}F_{12}\partial_{2\beta}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{23}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}F_{31}-\partial_{1\sigma}\partial_{3\gamma}F_{13}F_{32}\partial_{2\beta}\partial_{1}^{\sigma}F_{21})

The Ward identity (
C.14
) now becomes:

\displaystyle\bigg(8b_{1}-\frac{1}{4}b_{3}+8b_{1}+\frac{3}{4}b_{3}\bigg)A_{1}+\bigg(8b_{1}+\frac{1}{4}b_{3}\bigg)A_{2}+\bigg(16b_{2}-\frac{1}{2}b_{3}\bigg)A_{3}=0

(C.19)

Since
A_{1,2,3}
are linearly independent for generic
x_{1,2,3}
, the equation can only be satisfied if all the coefficients are zero. This leads to
b_{2}=-b_{1}
and
b_{3}=32b_{2}=-32b_{1}
, which is consistent with constraints obtained in (
2.27
) and other FB Ward identities.

C.3
Free/critical theory three-point functions

The correlators depend on three momenta
p_{1,2,3}^{\mu}
satisfying
p_{1}^{\mu}+p_{2}^{\mu}+p_{3}^{\mu}=0
. We denote the modulus of each vector by
p_{i}=\sqrt{p_{i}^{\mu}p_{i,\mu}}
. By using dimensional regularization, one can compute the following integrals:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\langle}J_{s_{1}}(p_{1})J_{s_{2}}(p_{2})J_{s_{3}}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}=\\
&\frac{N}{2}\int\frac{d^{3}\vec{k}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\bigg(\frac{V^{FB}_{s_{1}}(p_{1},k-p_{2})V^{FB}_{s_{2}}(p_{2},k)V^{FB}_{s_{3}}(p_{3},k+p_{3})}{(\vec{k}-\vec{p}_{2})^{2}\vec{k}^{2}(\vec{k}+\vec{p}_{3})^{2}}+\begin{Bmatrix}p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}\\
s_{2}\leftrightarrow s_{3}\end{Bmatrix}\bigg)\\
\\
&{\langle}J_{s_{1}}(p_{1})J_{s_{2}}(p_{2})J_{s_{3}}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}=\\
&-\frac{iN}{2}\int\frac{d^{3}\vec{k}}{(2\pi)^{3}}Tr\bigg[\frac{(\not{k}-\not{p_{2}})V^{FF}_{s_{1}}(p_{1},k-p_{2})(\not{k}+\not{p_{3}})V^{FF}_{s_{2}}(p_{2},k)\not{k}V^{FF}_{s_{3}}(p_{3},k+p_{3})}{(\vec{k}-\vec{p}_{2})^{2}\vec{k}^{2}(\vec{k}+\vec{p}_{3})^{2}}+\begin{Bmatrix}p_{2}\leftrightarrow p_{3}\\
s_{2}\leftrightarrow s_{3}\end{Bmatrix}\bigg]\end{aligned}

(C.20)

The vertex functions
V_{s}(p,k)
of currents in the FB, FF theories are obtained by Fourier transforming the position space operators in (
1.2
) and (
1.4
), respectively.
p
is the momentum of the insertion, while
k
is the outgoing loop momentum. We’ve included a
\frac{1}{2}
factor to reflect the fact that the correlators are normalized as in one-half of those in the theories of a single complex boson/Dirac fermion. We use this normalization in Sections
4
and
5
. Correlators of the CB theory can be obtained via FB correlators using the Legendre transformation (
3.2
).

Of special interest are three-point functions with one or two scalar currents, as in this case the full QF correlators can be obtained from free/critical theory computations. For the three-point function of one scalar current and two vector currents, we find:

\displaystyle{\langle}O(p_{1})J^{\mu}(p_{2})J^{\nu}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{2}^{\mu}{\epsilon}^{\nu p_{2}p_{3}}}{8p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}+\frac{p_{2}^{\nu}{\epsilon}^{\mu p_{2}p_{3}}}{8p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}+\frac{p_{2}{\epsilon}^{\mu\nu p_{3}}}{8(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}-\frac{p_{3}{\epsilon}^{\mu\nu p_{2}}}{8(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{3}^{\nu}{\epsilon}^{\mu p_{2}p_{3}}}{8p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}-\frac{p_{3}^{\mu}{\epsilon}^{\nu p_{2}p_{3}}}{8p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}+\frac{\left(\vec{p_{2}}\cdot\vec{p_{3}}\right){\epsilon}^{\mu\nu p_{2}}}{8p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}-\frac{\left(\vec{p_{2}}\cdot\vec{p_{3}}\right){\epsilon}^{\mu\nu p_{3}}}{8p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}

(C.21)

\displaystyle{\langle}O(p_{1})J^{\mu}(p_{2})J^{\nu}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}=

\displaystyle\frac{2\delta^{\mu\nu}}{p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}}+\frac{2p_{3}p_{2}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}}{p_{1}p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}+\frac{p_{3}p_{2}^{\mu}p_{3}^{\nu}}{p_{1}p_{2}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}+\frac{p_{2}p_{2}^{\mu}p_{3}^{\nu}}{p_{1}p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}

\displaystyle-\frac{p_{1}p_{2}^{\mu}p_{3}^{\nu}}{p_{2}p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}-\frac{2p_{2}^{\nu}p_{3}^{\mu}}{p_{1}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}+\frac{2p_{2}p_{3}^{\mu}p_{3}^{\nu}}{p_{1}p_{3}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}\par

(C.22)

The CB correlator is obtained by (
3.2
) as
{\langle}O(p_{1})J^{\mu}(p_{2})J^{\nu}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}=\frac{p_{1}}{8}{\langle}O(p_{1})J^{\mu}(p_{2})J^{\nu}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}
. It is interesting to analyze the
U(1)
Ward identities corresponding to these correlators. We have:

\displaystyle{\langle}O(p_{1})(ip_{2}\cdot J)(p_{2})J^{\nu}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}=0;\qquad{\langle}O(p_{1})(ip_{2}\cdot J)(p_{2})J^{\nu}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}=\frac{ip_{2}^{\nu}}{p_{1}}=\frac{ip_{2}^{\nu}}{4}{\langle}O(p_{1})O(-p_{1}){\rangle}_{FB}

(C.23)

Naively, we expect both expressions to be equal to zero, as the scalar and vector currents are not charged under the global
U(1)
symmetry. However the Ward identity of the FB theory contains an additional contact term, corresponding to an appearance of the nonlinear source term
\propto\mathcal{A}_{1\mu}\mathcal{A}^{1\mu}O
in the generating functional of the theory, where
\mathcal{A}_{1\mu}
is the background field coupled to the vector current. The
{\langle}JJO{\rangle}
correlators of FF and CB theories satisfy satisfy the epsilon-transform relation as shown in
[
39
]
, up to a contact term that can be removed by a suitable regularization scheme. We find:

\displaystyle{\langle}O(p_{1})J^{\mu}(p_{2})J^{\nu}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{CB}={\langle}O(p_{1})({\epsilon}\cdot J)^{\mu}(p_{2})J^{\nu}(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}-\frac{\delta^{\mu\nu}}{8}

(C.24)

After subtracting this contact term, one can contract the Lorentz indices with polarization vectors
(z^{\pm}_{p_{1}})^{\mu}(z^{\pm}_{p_{2}})^{\nu}
to write the QF correlator in spinor-helicity variables:

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} {\langle}J^{+}J^{-}O{\rangle}_{QF}=0;\qquad\qquad{\langle}J^{\pm}J^{\pm}O{\rangle}_{QF}={\tilde{N}}\sqrt{\frac{1\mp i{\tilde{\lambda}}}{1\pm i{\tilde{\lambda}}}}{\langle}J^{\pm}J^{\pm}O{\rangle}_{FF}\end{aligned}

(C.25)

For a more comprehensive overview of the spinor-helicity basis, we refer the reader to
[
52
,
43
]
.

Similarly, we find three-point functions involving the stress tensor and two scalars. The computation yields:

\displaystyle{\langle}T^{\mu\nu}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FF}=

\displaystyle\frac{-3p_{1}^{2}-2p_{1}(p_{2}+p_{3})+(p_{2}-p_{3})^{2}}{32p_{1}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}\left(p_{2}^{\mu}p_{3}^{\nu}+p_{2}^{\nu}p_{3}^{\mu}\right)+p_{2}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}\frac{(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})^{2}}{32p_{1}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}

\displaystyle+p_{3}^{\mu}p_{3}^{\nu}\frac{(p_{1}-p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}{32p_{1}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}+\left(\frac{p_{2}p_{3}}{8(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}+\frac{p_{1}}{32}-\frac{p_{2}}{24}-\frac{p_{3}}{24}\right)\delta^{\mu\nu}

(C.26)

\displaystyle{\langle}T^{\mu\nu}(p_{1})O(p_{2})O(p_{3}){\rangle}_{FB}=

\displaystyle\frac{8}{p_{2}}\frac{8}{p_{3}}\bigg[\frac{-3p_{1}^{2}-2p_{1}(p_{2}+p_{3})+(p_{2}-p_{3})^{2}}{32p_{1}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}\left(p_{2}^{\mu}p_{3}^{\nu}+p_{2}^{\nu}p_{3}^{\mu}\right)+p_{2}^{\mu}p_{2}^{\nu}\frac{(p_{1}+p_{2}-p_{3})^{2}}{32p_{1}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}

\displaystyle+p_{3}^{\mu}p_{3}^{\nu}\frac{(p_{1}-p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}{32p_{1}(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})^{2}}+\left(\frac{p_{2}p_{3}}{8(p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3})}+\frac{p_{1}}{32}-\frac{p_{2}}{24}-\frac{p_{3}}{24}\right)\delta^{\mu\nu}\bigg]\par

(C.27)

Conformal symmetry constrains the correlator
{\langle}TOO{\rangle}_{QF}
to a single, parity-even structure (
4.6
), fully determined by the number of dimensions and the scaling dimension of the scalar. This structure should be the same in both the
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to 0
and
{\tilde{\lambda}}\to\infty
limits, and indeed after Legendre-transforming the FB result (
C.27
), we recover the FF result (
C.26
), even up to contact terms. By contracting the result with polarization vectors
(z^{\pm}_{p_{1}})^{\mu}(z^{\pm}_{p_{1}})^{\nu}
, we can write
{\langle}TOO{\rangle}_{QF}
in spinor-helicity variables:

\displaystyle{\langle}T^{\pm\pm}OO{\rangle}_{QF}=A\pm iB

(C.28)

The positive- and negative-helicity components are consistently related by complex conjugation. Similarly, three-point correlators with four or more Lorentz indices can be computed by solving more complicated Feynman integrals.

C.4
Spin-
s^{+}
vertex in the collinear limit

The computation of vertex factors for
(J_{s})^{++...+}
in the collinear limit
q^{\pm}=0
follows the analogous computation for the spin-0 and spin-1 vertices of
[
23
]
. For simplicity, we will first compute the stress-tensor vertex, defined as:

\displaystyle{\langle}T^{++}(-q)\psi_{i}(k){\bar{\psi}}^{j}(-p){\rangle}=V^{++}(q,p)\delta^{j}_{i}(2\pi)^{3}\delta^{(3)}(q+p-k)

(C.29)

The result can then easily be generalized to arbitrary
s
.
For operators with
s\geq 2
, one has to worry about external gluon lines connecting to the vertex. However, our choice of gauge
A_{-}=0
ensures that the vertex functions
V^{++}(q,p)=V_{--}(q,p)
do not receive any such corrections, making the calculation more tractable. In similar fashion, correlators involving only
O
,
J_{1}^{\pm}
and
J_{s}^{--...-}
(for
s\geq 2
) vertices are simplest to compute in the
A_{+}=0
gauge.

Figure 7
:
Schematic Schwinger-Dyson equation for spin-2 vertex. The circled cross denotes the insertion of the exact
T^{++}
vertex, while the un-circled cross represents the free theory vertex. The filled circle denotes the exact fermion propagator.

For the stress-tensor normalized as in (
1.4
), the bootstrap equation, schematically represented in Figure
7
, is given by:

\displaystyle V^{++}(q,p)=ip^{+}{\gamma}^{+}-2\pi i\lambda\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}{\gamma}^{[3}S(k+q)V^{++}(q,k)S(k){\gamma}^{+]}\frac{1}{(k-p)^{+}}

(C.30)

For a general
A=a_{\mu}{\gamma}^{\mu}+a_{0}\mathds{1}
we have
{\gamma}^{[3}A{\gamma}^{+]}=2a_{0}{\gamma}^{+}-2a_{-}\mathds{1}
, so for the solution to be self-consistent, we see that
V^{++}(q,p)
does not have terms proportional to
{\gamma}^{-}
and
{\gamma}^{3}
. We assume a general ansatz for the vertex, based on dimensional analysis and rotational invariance in the
p^{+-}
plane:

\displaystyle V^{++}(q,p)=p^{+}v_{+}\bigg(\frac{2p_{s}}{|q_{3}|}\bigg){\gamma}^{+}+v_{1}\bigg(\frac{2p_{s}}{|q_{3}|}\bigg)\frac{(p^{+})^{2}}{q_{3}}\mathds{1}

(C.31)

With
v_{+}\big(\frac{2p_{s}}{|q_{3}|}\big)
and
v_{1}\big(\frac{2p_{s}}{|q_{3}|}\big)
being dimensionless functions. The vertex equation (
C.30
) can now be expanded using the ansatz and the fermion propagator (
5.2
). We find:

\displaystyle p^{+}v_{+}-ip^{+}=

\displaystyle 4\pi i\lambda\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{(k^{+})^{2}}{(k+q)^{2}k^{2}(k-p)^{+}}\bigg[(q_{3}+2i\lambda k_{s})v_{+}-\bigg((2\lambda^{2}-1)\frac{k_{s}^{2}}{q_{3}}-\frac{k_{3}}{q_{3}}(k_{3}+q_{3})\bigg)v_{1}\bigg]

(C.32)

\displaystyle\frac{(p^{+})^{2}}{q_{3}}v_{i}=

\displaystyle-4\pi i\lambda\int\frac{d^{3}k}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{(k^{+})^{3}}{(k+q)^{2}k^{2}(k-p)^{+}}\bigg[2v_{+}+\bigg(2i\lambda\frac{k_{s}}{q_{3}}-1\bigg)v_{1}\bigg]

(C.33)

We’ve suppressed the arguments of
v_{1}
,
v_{+}
for brevity. The integrals over
k_{3}
and the polar angle
\theta
in the
p^{+-}
plane can now be performed straightforwardly, since the functions
v_{1}
,
v_{+}
on the right-hand side of the equations depend only on
\big(\frac{2k_{s}}{|q_{3}|}\big)
. The remaining integral over
k_{s}
introduces a UV cutoff
\Lambda
. It is convenient to rewrite the resulting equations in terms of "parity-invariant" variables:

x=\frac{2k_{s}}{|q_{3}|}
,
y=\frac{2p_{s}}{|q_{3}|}
,
\Lambda^{\prime}=\frac{2\Lambda}{|q_{3}|}
and
\hat{\lambda}=\lambda\text{sign}(q_{3})
:

\displaystyle v_{+}(y)=

\displaystyle i-i\hat{\lambda}\int_{y}^{\Lambda^{\prime}}dx\frac{1}{1+x^{2}}\bigg(\frac{\hat{\lambda}^{2}-1}{2}x^{2}v_{1}(x)-(1+i\hat{\lambda}x)v_{+}(x)\bigg)

(C.34)

\displaystyle v_{1}(y)=

\displaystyle-i\hat{\lambda}\int_{y}^{\Lambda^{\prime}}dx\frac{2v_{+}(x)+(i\hat{\lambda}x-1)v_{1}(x)}{1+x^{2}}

(C.35)

These equations can be solved to give:

\displaystyle v_{1}(y)=

\displaystyle i(1-e^{-2i\hat{\lambda}(\arctan(y)-\arctan(\Lambda^{\prime}))})

(C.36)

\displaystyle v_{+}(y)=

\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}(1-iy\hat{\lambda}+e^{-2i\hat{\lambda}(\arctan(y)-\arctan(\Lambda^{\prime}))}(1+iy\hat{\lambda}))

(C.37)

Inserting this back into (
C.31
) the vertex function
V^{++}(q,p)
. Notably, the relation between spin-1 and spin-2 vertices in the collinear limit is given by
V^{++}(q,p)=p^{+}V^{+}(q,p)
. To generalize the result to spin-
s
currents, note that the free theory vertex in the bootstrap equation (
C.30
) in the collinear limit is modified by
(p^{+})^{s-1}
and similarly, Lorentz invariance requires us to modify the ansatz (
C.31
) by the same factor. The computation carries through identically, and we find:

\displaystyle V_{s}^{++...+}(q,p)=\beta_{s}(p^{+})^{s-1}V^{+}(q,p)

(C.38)

Where
\beta_{s}
is just an overall normalization factor. In our conventions (
1.4
) we have
\beta_{2}=1
and
\beta_{3}=8i/3
.

An analogous relation was observed in
[
23
]
when computing vertex functions in the QB Chern-Simons-Matter theory.
